\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta}_{I_{1}}-\underbrace{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\partial_{\psi}(a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u})\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta}_{I_{2}}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\underbrace{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}[F(\theta,\psi)-b(\theta,\psi)\tilde{u}-c(\theta,\psi)\tilde{v}]\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta}_{I_{3}}.

Obviously,
I_{1}=0
. Due to the fast decay of
b(\theta,\psi),c(\theta,\psi)
as
\psi\rightarrow-\infty
, we deduce that

\displaystyle|I_{3}|\leq

\displaystyle C\|F\big<\psi\big>^{l+1}\|_{2}\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2}+C(\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{2}+\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|F\big<\psi\big>^{l+1}\|_{2}^{2}+C\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2}^{2}.

Moreover, there holds

\displaystyle I_{2}=\underbrace{\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta}_{I_{21}}+\underbrace{2l\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{2l-1}d\psi d\theta}_{I_{22}}.

Notice that
a(\theta,\psi)=u_{e}(1)+u_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Y(\theta,\psi))
, we deduce that

\displaystyle I_{21}=

\displaystyle u_{e}(1)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta+\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}u_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Y(\theta,\psi))\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle u_{e}(1)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta+\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}u_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Y(\theta,\psi))\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{l}\|^{2}_{2}-C\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{2}.

Moreover, by the Hölder inequality and the Poincaré inequality, there holds

\displaystyle|I_{22}|\leq C(l)\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{l}\|_{2}\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{l}\|_{2}\leq C(l)\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2}+C\|F\big<\psi\big>^{l+1}\|_{2}.

(2.119)

Noticing

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}-a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}=F(\theta,\psi)+\partial_{\psi}a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}-b(\theta,\psi)\tilde{u}-c(\theta,\psi)\tilde{v},

we deduce that

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}-a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}]\psi^{l}\|_{2}^{2}=\|[F(\theta,\psi)+\partial_{\psi}a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}-b(\theta,\psi)\tilde{u}-c(\theta,\psi)\tilde{v}]\psi^{l}\|_{2}^{2}.

The right side can be controlled by

\displaystyle C\|F\big<\psi\big>^{l}\|_{2}^{2}+C(\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{2}+\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{2}).

Moreover, there holds

\displaystyle\|[\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}-a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}]\psi^{l}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}^{2}+\|a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}^{2}-2\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\alpha}{2}\|\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}^{2}\underbrace{-2\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta}_{I}.

Integrating by parts, we deduce that

\displaystyle I=

\displaystyle\underbrace{2\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\partial_{\psi}a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta}_{I_{1}}+\underbrace{4l\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{2l-1}d\psi d\theta}_{I_{2}}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{2\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}a(\theta,\psi)\partial_{\theta\psi}\tilde{u}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{2l}d\psi d\theta}_{I_{3}}.

Obviously, there holds

\displaystyle|I_{1}|+|I_{3}|\leq C(\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{2}+\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{2}).

Moreover,

\displaystyle|I_{2}|=

\displaystyle\Big|4lu_{e}(1)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{2l-1}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}d\psi d\theta+4l\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}u_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Y(\theta,\psi))\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{2l-1}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}d\psi d\theta\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|4lu_{e}(1)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{2l-1}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}d\psi d\theta+4l\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{-\infty}^{0}u_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Y(\theta,\psi))\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{2l-1}\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}d\psi d\theta\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(l)\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2}+C(l)(\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{2}+\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\|^{2}_{2}).

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}+\|\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}\leq C(l)\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2}+C(l)\|F\big<\psi\big>^{l+1}\|_{2}.

(2.120)

Combining the estimates (
2.119
) and (
2.120
), we obtain that for any
l\geq 1
, there holds

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}+\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{l}\|_{2}+\|\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}\leq C(l)(\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}_{\neq}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2}+\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2})+C(l)\|F\big<\psi\big>^{l}\|_{2}.

Thus, by induction, we obtain (
2.118
).

Furthermore, by the Hardy inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2}\leq C\|\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2},

hence there holds

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}+\|\partial_{\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{l-1}\|_{2}+\|\partial_{\psi\psi}\tilde{u}\psi^{l}\|_{2}\leq C\|F\big<\psi\big>^{l+1}\|_{2}.

Returning to the original variable, we obtain that for any
l\geq 1
, there holds

\displaystyle\big\|\big<Y\big>^{l}\partial_{YY}u\big\|_{2}^{2}+\|\big<Y\big>^{l-1}\partial_{\theta}u\|^{2}_{2}+\|\big<Y\big>^{l-1}\partial_{Y}u\|^{2}_{2}\leq C(l)\|\big<Y\big>^{l+1}\tilde{f}\|^{2}_{2}.

Furthermore, by induction, we obtain that for any
m\in\mathbb{N}_{+},l\in\mathbb{N}
, there holds

\displaystyle\sum_{j+k\leq m}\Big(\big\|\big<Y\big>^{l}\partial^{j}_{\theta}\partial^{k}_{Y}\partial_{YY}u\big\|_{2}^{2}+\big\|\big<Y\big>^{l-1}\partial^{j}_{\theta}\partial^{k}_{Y}\partial_{\theta}u\big\|_{2}^{2}+\big\|\big<Y\big>^{l-1}\partial^{j}_{\theta}\partial^{k}_{Y}\partial_{Y}u\big\|_{2}^{2}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C(m,l)\sum\limits_{j+k\leq m}\big\|\big<Y\big>^{l+1}\partial^{j}_{\theta}\partial^{k}_{Y}\tilde{f}\big\|^{2}_{2}\leq C(m,l)\eta^{2}.

Noticing that
\lim\limits_{Y\rightarrow-\infty}(u_{\theta},u_{Y})=0
and
A_{1\infty}:=\lim\limits_{Y\rightarrow-\infty}u(\theta,Y)
is a constant independent on
\theta
, then by the Hardy inequality we have for any
l\geq 2

\displaystyle\|Y^{l-2}(u-A_{1\infty})\|_{2}\leq C(l)\|Y^{l-1}\partial_{Y}u\|_{2}\leq C(l)\eta^{2}.

This completes the proof of this proposition.

MM

\square

Next, we construct the pressure
p_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)
. Consider the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}p_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)=g_{1}(\theta,Y),\quad\lim_{Y\rightarrow-\infty}p_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)=0,

(2.121)

where

\displaystyle g_{1}(\theta,Y)=

\displaystyle-Y\partial_{Y}p_{p}^{(1)}+\partial_{YY}v_{p}^{(1)}-u_{e}(1)\partial_{\theta}v_{p}^{(1)}-u_{p}^{(0)}(\partial_{\theta}v_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,1)+\partial_{\theta}v_{p}^{(1)})

\displaystyle-\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(1)}(v_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,1)+v_{p}^{(1)})-2Yu^{\prime}_{e}(1)u_{p}^{(0)}+u_{e}(1)\tilde{u}_{p}^{(1)}+[u_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,1)+A_{1}]u_{p}^{(0)}+u_{p}^{(0)}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(1)},

here and below,

\tilde{u}_{p}^{(1)}=u_{p}^{(1)}-A_{1\infty}.

g_{1}(\theta,Y)
can be obtained by replacing
u_{p}^{(1)}
by
\tilde{u}_{p}^{(1)}
in the expansion (
2.11
) and putting the new expansion into the second equation of (
1
), then collecting the
\varepsilon^{1}
-order terms together.
Notice that
g_{1}(\theta,Y)
decay fast as
Y\rightarrow-\infty
, we can get
p_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)
by solving (
2.11
) and deduce that
p_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)
decay fast as
Y\rightarrow-\infty
.

By the same argument as above, we can obtain the well-posedness of equations (
2.3.3
).

Proposition 2.7
.

There exists
\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, the equations (
2.3.3
) have a unique solution
(\hat{u}_{p}^{(1)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(2)})
which satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sum_{j+k\leq m}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{Z}^{k}\big(\hat{u}_{p}^{(1)}-\hat{A}_{1\infty},v_{p}^{(2)}\big)\big|^{2}\big<Z\big>^{2l}d\theta dZ\leq C(m,l)\eta^{2},\ \forall m,l\geq 0,\\
&\int_{0}^{2\pi}\hat{v}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Z)d\theta=0,\ \forall Z\geq 0,\end{aligned}

(2.122)

where

\hat{A}_{1\infty}:=\lim\limits_{Z\rightarrow+\infty}\hat{u}_{p}^{(1)}(\theta,Z)
is a constant which satisfies
|\hat{A}_{1\infty}|\leq C\eta.

Similarly, we can also construct
\hat{p}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Z)
which decays fast as
Z\rightarrow+\infty
.

2.4.4.
Linearized Euler system for
(u_{e}^{(2)},v_{e}^{(2)},p_{e}^{(2)})
and its solvability

Let
r_{1}=\frac{1+2r_{0}}{3},r_{2}=\frac{2+r_{0}}{3}
and
\chi(r)\in C^{\infty}([r_{0},1])
be an increasing smooth function such that

\displaystyle\chi(r)=\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}0,\ r\in[r_{0},r_{1}],\\[5.0pt]
1,\ r\in[r_{2},1]\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r):

\displaystyle=u_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\chi(r)A_{1\infty}+(1-\chi(r))\hat{A}_{1\infty},\ \bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)=v_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r),

\displaystyle\bar{p}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r):

\displaystyle=p_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{2u_{e}(s)}{s}[\chi(s)A_{1\infty}+(1-\chi(s))\hat{A}_{1\infty}]ds,

then
(\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)},\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)},\bar{p}_{e}^{(1)})
also satisfies the linearized Euler equations (
2.1.2
)
with the boundary condition (
2.10
).

Putting

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=u_{e}(r)+\varepsilon\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\varepsilon^{2}u_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=\varepsilon\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\varepsilon^{2}v_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle p^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=p_{e}(r)+\varepsilon\bar{p}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\varepsilon^{2}p_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+\text{h.o.t.}

into the Navier-Stokes equations (
1
), we obtain the following linearized Euler equations for
(u_{e}^{(2)},v_{e}^{(2)},p_{e}^{(2)})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}u_{e}(r)\partial_{\theta}u_{e}^{(2)}+rv_{e}^{(2)}u^{\prime}_{e}(r)+u_{e}(r)v_{e}^{(2)}+\partial_{\theta}p_{e}^{(2)}+\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\\[5.0pt]
\quad\quad\quad\quad+\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}=ru^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)+u^{\prime}_{e}(r)-\frac{u_{e}(r)}{r}+rF_{u},\\[5.0pt]
u_{e}(r)\partial_{\theta}v_{e}^{(2)}-2u_{e}(r)u_{e}^{(2)}+r\partial_{r}p_{e}^{(2)}+\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}+\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}-(\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)})^{2}=rF_{v},\\[7.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}u_{e}^{(2)}+\partial_{r}(rv_{e}^{(2)})=0,\end{array}\right.

with the boundary conditions

\displaystyle v_{e}^{(2)}|_{r=1}=-v_{p}^{(2)}|_{Y=0},\ v_{e}^{(2)}|_{r=r_{0}}=-\hat{v}_{p}^{(2)}|_{Z=0},\ v_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)=v_{e}^{(2)}(\theta+2\pi,r).

(2.128)

Proposition 2.8
.

The linearized Euler system (
2.4.4
) has a solution
(u_{e}^{(2)},v_{e}^{(2)},p_{e}^{(2)})
which satisfies

\displaystyle\|\partial^{k}_{\theta}\partial^{j}_{r}(u_{e}^{(2)},v_{e}^{(2)})\|_{2}\leq C(j,k),\ \forall j,k\geq 0.

(2.129)

Proof.

First, we show that there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}[\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}]d\theta=0,\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1].

(2.130)

We set

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)=\sum_{n\neq 0}V_{n}(r)e^{in\theta},\ \bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)=a(r)-\sum_{n\neq 0}\frac{(rV_{n})^{\prime}(r)}{in}e^{in\theta},

here we have used (
2.96
) and the third equation of (
2.1.2
).

Then, it’s easy to obtain that

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)

\displaystyle=(ra^{\prime}(r)+a(r))\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)-\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)\sum_{n\neq 0}\frac{e^{in\theta}}{in}[r(rV_{n})^{\prime\prime}+(rV_{n})^{\prime}].

(2.131)

Moreover, due to
-\triangle(r\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)})+U_{e}(r)(r\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)})=0
, where
U_{e}(r)=\frac{1}{u_{e}(r)}\Big(u^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)+\frac{u^{\prime}_{e}(r)}{r}-\frac{u_{e}(r)}{r^{2}}\Big),

we deduce that

\displaystyle\sum_{n\neq 0}e^{in\theta}[r^{2}(rV_{n})^{\prime\prime}+r(rV_{n})^{\prime}-(n^{2}+r^{2}U_{e}(r))rV_{n}]=0,

hence there holds
r(rV_{n})^{\prime\prime}+(rV_{n})^{\prime}=(n^{2}+r^{2}U_{e}(r))V_{n}
.

Thus, by (
2.131
) we deduce that

\displaystyle\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(ra^{\prime}(r)+a(r))\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)\Big(\sum_{n\neq 0}ine^{in\theta}V_{n}-r^{2}U_{e}(r)\sum_{n\neq 0}\frac{e^{in\theta}}{in}V_{n}\Big)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(ra^{\prime}(r)+a(r))\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)\partial_{\theta}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)-\frac{r^{2}U_{e}(r)}{2}\partial_{\theta}\Big(\sum_{n\neq 0}\frac{e^{in\theta}}{in}V_{n}\Big)^{2},

(2.132)

hence (
2.130
) can be obtained.

Then, we can follow the line of the construction of
(u_{e}^{(1)},v_{e}^{(1)},p_{e}^{(1)})
to construct
(u_{e}^{(2)},v_{e}^{(2)},p_{e}^{(2)})
: eliminating pressure
p_{e}^{(2)}
in the equation (
2.4.4
) and obtaining the elliptic equation for
rv_{e}^{(2)}
:

\displaystyle-u_{e}(r)\triangle(rv_{e}^{(2)})+\Big(u^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)+\frac{u^{\prime}_{e}(r)}{r}-\frac{u_{e}(r)}{r^{2}}\Big)(rv_{e}^{(2)})

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-r\partial_{r}\big(\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}\big)+\partial_{\theta}\big(\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}+\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)}-(\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)})^{2}\big)

\displaystyle+r\partial_{r}\Big(ru^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)+u^{\prime}_{e}(r)-\frac{u_{e}(r)}{r}+rF_{u}\Big)-\partial_{\theta}(rF_{v}).

This elliptic equation with boundary condition (
2.128
) is solvable by noticing (
1.12
) and (
2.130
). Then
u_{e}^{(2)}
can be obtained from the divergence-free condition; finally, construct pressure
p_{e}^{(2)}
from the first equation in (
2.4.4
). Thus,
(u_{e}^{(2)},v_{e}^{(2)},p_{e}^{(2)})
satisfies the equation (
2.4.4
) and (
2.129
).

MM

\square

Remark 2.1
.

In this remark, we show that the solvability of (
2.4.4
) also determine the form of
u_{e}(r)
. In fact, integrating the first equation of (
2.4.4
) with respect to
\theta
in
[0,2\pi]
, notice
\int_{0}^{2\pi}v_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)d\theta=0
and (
2.130
), we deduce that

u^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)+\frac{u^{\prime}_{e}(r)}{r}-\frac{u_{e}(r)}{r^{2}}+\bar{F}_{u}=0,

this is the equation in (
1.12
).

Let
A_{1}(r)
be a smooth function such that

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}rA^{\prime\prime}_{1}(r)+A^{\prime}_{1}(r)-\frac{A_{1}(r)}{r}\\[5.0pt]
\quad\quad\quad=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(v^{(1)}_{e}\partial_{r}(ru^{(2)}_{e})+v^{(2)}_{e}\partial_{r}(r\bar{u}^{(1)}_{e})-\Big(r\partial_{rr}\bar{u}^{(1)}_{e}+\partial_{r}\bar{u}^{(1)}_{e}-\frac{\bar{u}^{(1)}_{e}}{r}\Big)\Big)d\theta,\\[10.0pt]
A_{1}(r_{0})=A_{1}(1)=0,\end{array}\right.

then
\|\partial_{r}^{k}A_{1}(r)\|_{\infty}\leq C(k)\eta.

Set

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}=\bar{u}_{e}^{(1)}+A_{1}(r),\ \tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}=\bar{v}_{e}^{(1)},\ \tilde{p}_{e}^{(1)}=\bar{p}_{e}^{(1)}+\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{2u_{e}(s)}{s}A_{1}(s)ds,

(2.136)

then
(\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)},\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)},\tilde{p}_{e}^{(1)})
is also a solution of (
2.1.2
) with the boundary condition (
2.10
), and there holds

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k}(\tilde{u}^{(1)}_{e},\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)})\|_{\infty}\leq C(j,k)\eta,\ \forall k,j\geq 0.

(2.137)

Thus, by the same argument as Proposition
2.8
, we deduce that the following linearized Euler system

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}u_{e}(r)\partial_{\theta}u_{e}^{(2)}+rv_{e}^{(2)}u^{\prime}_{e}(r)+u_{e}(r)v_{e}^{(2)}+\partial_{\theta}p_{e}^{(2)}+\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}\\[5.0pt]
\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}=ru^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)+u^{\prime}_{e}(r)-\frac{u_{e}(r)}{r}+rF_{\theta},\\[5.0pt]
u_{e}(r)\partial_{\theta}v_{e}^{(2)}-2u_{e}(r)u_{e}^{(2)}+r\partial_{r}p_{e}^{(2)}+\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}+\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}-(\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)})^{2}=rF_{r},\\[7.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}u_{e}^{(2)}+r\partial_{r}v_{e}^{(2)}+v_{e}^{(2)}=0\end{array}\right.

with the boundary conditions

\displaystyle v_{e}^{(2)}|_{r=1}=-v_{p}^{(2)}|_{Y=0},\ v_{e}^{(2)}|_{r=r_{0}}=-\hat{v}_{p}^{(2)}|_{Z=0},\ v_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)=v_{e}^{(2)}(\theta+2\pi,r),

(2.143)

has a solution
(\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)},\bar{v}_{e}^{(2)},\bar{p}_{e}^{(2)})
which satisfies

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k}(\bar{u}^{(2)}_{e},\bar{v}_{e}^{(2)})\|_{\infty}\leq C(j,k).

(2.144)

2.4.5.
Linearized Prandtl equations for
(u_{p}^{(2)},v_{p}^{(3)})
,
(\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)})
and their solvabilities

Let

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=u_{e}(r)+u_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Y)+\varepsilon\big[\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\tilde{u}_{p}^{(1)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\varepsilon^{2}\big[\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+u_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=\varepsilon\big[\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+v_{p}^{(1)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\varepsilon^{2}\big[\bar{v}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+v_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\varepsilon^{3}[v_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,r)+v_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)]+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle p^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=p_{e}(r)+\varepsilon\big[\tilde{p}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+p_{p}^{(1)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\varepsilon\big[\bar{p}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+p_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\varepsilon^{3}p_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)+\text{h.o.t.},

with the boundary conditions

\displaystyle\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,1)+u_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,0)=0,\ v_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,1)+v_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,0)=0,\ \lim_{Y\rightarrow-\infty}(\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(2)},v_{p}^{(3)})(\theta,Y)=(0,0),

we obtain the following linearized steady Prandtl equations for
(u_{p}^{(2)},v_{p}^{(3)})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(u_{e}(1)+u_{p}^{(0)}\big)\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{(2)}+\big(v_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,1)+v_{p}^{(1)}\big)\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(2)}+(v_{p}^{(3)}-v_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,0))\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(0)}\\[5.0pt]
\quad\quad\quad\quad+(u_{p}^{(2)}+\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,1))\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{(0)}-\partial_{YY}u_{p}^{(2)}=f_{2}(\theta,Y),\\[5.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{(2)}+\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(3)}+\partial_{Y}(Yv_{p}^{(2)})=0,\\[5.0pt]
u_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)=u_{p}^{(2)}(\theta+2\pi,Y),\ v_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)=v_{p}^{(3)}(\theta+2\pi,Y),\\[5.0pt]
u_{p}^{(2)}\big|_{Y=0}=-\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)}\big|_{r=1},\ \lim\limits_{Y\rightarrow-\infty}\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)=\lim\limits_{Y\rightarrow-\infty}v_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)=0\end{array}\right.

here

\displaystyle f_{2}(\theta,Y)=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\theta}p_{p}^{(2)}+Y\partial_{YY}u_{p}^{(1)}+\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(1)}+\partial_{\theta\theta}u_{p}^{(0)}-u_{p}^{(0)}-\tilde{u}_{p}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{(1)}-v_{p}^{(2)}\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(1)}-\sum_{i+j=2}v_{p}^{(i)}Y\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(j)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{2}\sum_{i+j=2-k,(k,j)\neq(0,2)}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}+\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\Big)+u_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,1)\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{(0)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{1}\sum_{i+j=2-k}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k+1}\partial_{Y}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}+v_{p}^{(i)}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}(r\partial_{r}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(j)})(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\Big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{2}\sum_{i+j=3-k,(k,j)\neq(0,2),(0,0)}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\partial_{Y}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)},

where
\tilde{u}_{p}^{(0)}=u_{p}^{(0)},\ \tilde{u}_{e}^{(0)}=u_{e}(r),\ \tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)}=\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)},\ \tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)}=\bar{v}_{e}^{(2)}.

Similarly, let

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=u_{e}(r)+\widehat{u}_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Z)+\varepsilon\big[\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+(\widehat{u}_{p}^{(1)}(\theta,Z)-\hat{A}_{1})\big]+\varepsilon^{2}\big[\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+\widehat{u}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Z)\big]+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=\varepsilon\big[\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\widehat{v}_{p}^{(1)}(\theta,Z)\big]+\varepsilon^{2}\big[\bar{v}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+\widehat{v}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Z)\big]+\varepsilon^{3}[v_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,r)+\widehat{v}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z)]+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle p^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=p_{e}(r)+\varepsilon\big[\tilde{p}_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r)+\widehat{p}_{p}^{(1)}(\theta,Z)\big]+\varepsilon^{2}\big[\tilde{p}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+\widehat{p}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Z)\big]+\varepsilon\widehat{p}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z)+\text{h.o.t.},

with the following boundary conditions

\displaystyle\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r_{0})+\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,0)=0,\ v_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,r_{0})+\widehat{v}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,0)=0,\ \lim_{Z\rightarrow+\infty}(\partial_{Z}\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)})(\theta,Z)=(0,0),

we obtain the following linearized Prandlt equations for
(\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(u_{e}(r_{0})+\hat{u}_{p}^{(0)}\big)\partial_{\theta}\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}+\big(v_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r_{0})+\hat{v}_{p}^{(1)}\big)r_{0}\partial_{Z}\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}+(\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}+\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r_{0}))\partial_{\theta}\hat{u}_{p}^{(0)}\\[5.0pt]
\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+(\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)}-\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,0))r_{0}\partial_{Z}\hat{u}_{p}^{(0)}-r_{0}\partial_{ZZ}\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}=\hat{f}_{2}(\theta,Z),\\[5.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}+r_{0}\partial_{Z}\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)}+\partial_{Z}(Z\hat{v}_{p}^{(2)})=0,\\[5.0pt]
\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Z)=\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta+2\pi,Z),\ \hat{v}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z)=\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta+2\pi,Z),\\[5.0pt]
\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}\big|_{Z=0}=-u_{e}^{(2)}\big|_{r=r_{0}},\ \lim\limits_{Z\rightarrow+\infty}(\partial_{Z}\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z))=(0,0).\end{array}\right.

The expressions of functions
\hat{f}_{2}(\theta,Z)
are similar to
f_{2}(\theta,Y)
, we omit the details here.

Proposition 2.9
.

There exists
\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, the equations (
2.4.5
) have a unique solution
(u_{p}^{(2)},v_{p}^{(3)})
and the equations (
2.4.5
) have a unique solution
(\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)})
which satisfies

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sum_{j+k\leq m}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{Y}^{k}(u_{p}^{(2)}-A_{2\infty},v_{p}^{(3)})\Big|^{2}\big<Y\big>^{2l}d\theta dY\leq C(m,l),\ \forall m,l\geq 0,\\
&\sum_{j+k\leq m}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{Z}^{k}(\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}-\hat{A}_{2\infty},\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)})\Big|^{2}\big<Z\big>^{2l}d\theta dZ\leq C(m,l),\ \forall m,l\geq 0,\\
&\int_{0}^{2\pi}v_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)d\theta=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z)d\theta=0,\ \forall\ Y\leq 0,\ Z\geq 0\end{aligned}

(2.157)

where

\displaystyle A_{2\infty}:=\lim_{Y\rightarrow-\infty}u_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y),\ \hat{A}_{2\infty}:=\lim_{Z\rightarrow+\infty}\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Z).

and satisfies
|A_{2\infty}|+|\hat{A}_{2\infty}|\leq C
.

The proof is same with Proposition
2.6
by noticing that
f_{2}(\theta,Y)
decays very fast as
Y\rightarrow-\infty
, we omit the details.

We construct the pressure
p_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)
by considering the equation

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}p_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)=g_{2}(\theta,Y),\ \lim_{Y\rightarrow-\infty}p_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)=0,

(2.158)

where

\displaystyle g_{2}(\theta,Y)=

\displaystyle\partial_{YY}v_{p}^{(2)}+Y\partial_{YY}v_{p}^{(1)}+\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(1)}-2\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{(0)}-Y\partial_{Y}p_{p}^{(2)}-\sum_{i+j=3}v_{p}^{(i)}\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(j)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i+j=2}\Big(\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\partial_{\theta}v_{p}^{(j)}+v_{p}^{(i)}Y\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(j)}-\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}+\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)Y\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(j)}+v_{p}^{(i)}\partial_{r}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(j)}(\theta,1)\Big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{1}\sum_{i+j=2-k}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\partial_{\theta}v_{p}^{(j)}+\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(j)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\Big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{2}\sum_{i+j=2-k}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}+\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(j)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\Big),

where
\tilde{u}_{p}^{(0)}=u_{p}^{(0)},\ \tilde{u}_{e}^{(0)}=u_{e}(r),\ \tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)}=\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)}+A_{2\infty},\ \tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)}=\bar{v}_{e}^{(2)}
, and here and below
\tilde{u}_{p}^{(2)}=u_{p}^{(2)}-A_{2\infty}
.
g_{2}(\theta,Y)
can be derived by the same argument as
g_{1}(\theta,Y)
.
Moreover, notice that
g_{2}(\theta,Y)
decay fast as
Y\rightarrow-\infty
, we can obtain
p_{p}^{(3)}
by solving (
2.158
) and
p_{p}^{(3)}
also decay fast as
Y\rightarrow-\infty
.

Similarly, we can construct
\hat{p}^{(3)}_{p}(\theta,Z)
and
\hat{p}^{(3)}_{p}(\theta,Z)
which also decay fast as
Z\rightarrow+\infty
.

2.4.6.
Linearized Euler system for
(u_{e}^{(3)},v_{e}^{(3)},p_{e}^{(3)})
and its solvability

Let

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r):

\displaystyle=\bar{u}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+\chi(r)A_{2\infty}+(1-\chi(r))\hat{A}_{2\infty},\ \tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)=\bar{v}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r),

\displaystyle\tilde{p}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r):

\displaystyle=\bar{p}_{e}^{(2)}(\theta,r)+\int_{r_{0}}^{r}\frac{2u_{e}(s)}{s}[\chi(s)A_{2\infty}+(1-\chi(s))\hat{A}_{2\infty}]ds,

then
(\tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)},\tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)},\tilde{p}_{e}^{(2)})
also satisfies the linearized Euler equations (
2.4.4
) with the boundary conditions (
2.143
), and there holds

\displaystyle\|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k}(\tilde{u}^{(2)}_{e},\tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)})\|_{\infty}\leq C(j,k),\ \forall k,j\geq 0.

(2.159)

Putting

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=u_{e}(r)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle p^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=p_{e}(r)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\tilde{p}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)+\text{h.o.t.},

into the Navier-Stokes equations (
1
), we find that
(\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)},\tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)},\tilde{p}_{e}^{(3)})
satisfies the following linearized Euler equations in
\Omega

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}u_{e}(r)\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}+r\tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)}u^{\prime}_{e}(r)+u_{e}(r)\tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)}+\partial_{\theta}\tilde{p}_{e}^{(3)}+f_{e}(\theta,r)=0,\\[5.0pt]
u_{e}(r)\partial_{\theta}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)}-2u_{e}(r)\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}+r\partial_{r}\tilde{p}_{e}^{(3)}+g_{e}(\theta,r)=0,\\[7.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}+\partial_{r}(r\tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)})=0,\end{array}\right.

equipped with the following boundary conditions

\displaystyle\tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)}|_{r=1}=-v_{p}^{(3)}|_{Y=0},\ \tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)}|_{r=r_{0}}=-\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)}|_{Z=0},\ \tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,r)=\tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)}(\theta+2\pi,r),

where

\displaystyle f_{e}(\theta,r)=

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)}+\tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)}+\tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)}r\partial_{r}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)}+\tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{\partial_{\theta\theta}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}}{r}+r\partial_{rr}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\partial_{r}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}-\frac{2}{r}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}-\frac{\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}}{r}\Big),

\displaystyle g_{e}(\theta,r)=

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)}+\tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}+\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}r\partial_{r}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)}+\tilde{v}_{e}^{(2)}r\partial_{r}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}-2\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(2)}

\displaystyle-\Big(\frac{\partial_{\theta\theta}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}}{r}+r\partial_{rr}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}+\partial_{r}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}-\frac{2}{r}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(1)}+\frac{\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}}{r}\Big).

Proposition 2.10
.

The linearized Euler equations (
2.4.6
) have a solution
(\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)},\tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)},\tilde{p}_{e}^{(3)})
which satisfies

\displaystyle\|\partial^{k}_{\theta}\partial^{j}_{r}(\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)},\tilde{v}_{e}^{(3)})\|_{2}\leq C(j,k),\ \forall j,k\geq 0.

(2.164)

Proof.

We only need to check
\int_{0}^{2\pi}f_{e}(\theta,r)d\theta=0
, that is,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(\tilde{v}^{(1)}_{e}\partial_{r}(r\tilde{u}^{(2)}_{e})+\tilde{v}^{(2)}_{e}\partial_{r}(r\tilde{u}^{(1)}_{e})-\Big(r\partial_{rr}\tilde{u}^{(1)}_{e}+\partial_{r}\tilde{u}^{(1)}_{e}-\frac{\tilde{u}^{(1)}_{e}}{r}\Big)\Big)d\theta=0,\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1].

(2.165)

By (
2.4.4
), we only need to prove

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(v^{(1)}_{e}\partial_{r}(ru^{(2)}_{e})+v^{(2)}_{e}\partial_{r}(r\bar{u}^{(1)}_{e})\Big)(\theta,r)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(\tilde{v}^{(1)}_{e}\partial_{r}(r\tilde{u}^{(2)}_{e})+\tilde{v}^{(2)}_{e}\partial_{r}(r\tilde{u}^{(1)}_{e})\Big)(\theta,r)d\theta,\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1].

Notice that
v_{e}^{(1)}=\tilde{v}_{e}^{(1)}
,
\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{(2)}_{e}d\theta=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\tilde{v}^{(2)}_{e}d\theta=0
and
\tilde{u}^{(1)}_{e}=\bar{u}^{(1)}_{e}+A_{1}(r)
, thus we only need to prove

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(v^{(1)}_{e}\partial_{r}(ru^{(2)}_{e}-r\tilde{u}^{(2)}_{e})+(v^{(2)}_{e}-\tilde{v}^{(2)}_{e})\partial_{r}(r\bar{u}^{(1)}_{e})\Big)(\theta,r)d\theta=0,\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1].

For simplicity, we write
[v^{(1)},u^{(1)}]=[v^{(1)}_{e},\bar{u}^{(1)}_{e}]
, and
[v^{(2)},u^{(2)}]=[v^{(2)}_{e}-\tilde{v}^{(2)}_{e},u^{(2)}_{e}-\tilde{u}^{(2)}_{e}]
. Notice that
v^{(1)}
satisfies equations (
2.4.2
), i.e.

\displaystyle-r\Delta(rv^{(1)})+Brv^{(1)}=0,

where
B=\frac{r}{u_{e}(r)}\Big(u^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)+\frac{u^{\prime}_{e}(r)}{r}-\frac{u_{e}(r)}{r^{2}}\Big)
is a radial function. By equations (
2.4.4
) and (
2.4.4
),
v^{(2)}
satisfies the following equation:

\displaystyle-r\Delta(rv^{(2)})+Brv^{(2)}=f^{(2)},

where

\displaystyle f^{(2)}=

\displaystyle\frac{r}{u_{e}}\Big[r^{2}v^{(1)}\Delta A_{1}-rA_{1}\Delta(rv^{(1)})-\frac{A_{1}}{r}v^{(1)}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{r}{u_{e}}\Big[r^{2}v^{(1)}\Delta A_{1}-rA_{1}Bv^{(1)}-\frac{A_{1}}{r}v^{(1)}\Big].

We set
I[h]=\sum\limits_{n\neq 0}\frac{e^{in\theta}}{in}h_{n}(r)
for
h
satisfying
\int_{0}^{2\pi}hd\theta=0
. Since
u^{(2)}_{\theta}=-(rv^{(2)})_{r}
, there holds

\displaystyle\Big(ru^{(2)}-\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{(2)}d\theta\Big)_{r}

\displaystyle=-I\Big[\big(r(rv^{(2)})_{r}\big)_{r}\Big]

\displaystyle=-I\Big[r\Delta(rv^{(2)})-\frac{v^{(2)}_{\theta\theta}}{r}\Big]=I[f^{(2)}]+\frac{v^{(2)}_{\theta}}{r}-rBI[v^{(2)}].

Similarly,

\displaystyle\Big(ru^{(1)}-\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{(1)}d\theta\Big)_{r}=\frac{v^{(1)}_{\theta}}{r}-rBI[v^{(1)}].

Thus we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(v^{(1)}_{e}\partial_{r}(ru^{(2)}_{e}-r\tilde{u}^{(2)}_{e})+(v^{(2)}_{e}-\tilde{v}^{(2)}_{e})\partial_{r}(r\bar{u}^{(1)}_{e})\Big)(\theta,r)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(v^{(1)}\partial_{r}(ru^{(2)})-v^{(2)}\partial_{r}(ru^{(1)})\Big)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(v^{(1)}I[f^{(2)}]+\frac{v^{(1)}v^{(2)}_{\theta}+v^{(2)}v^{(1)}_{\theta}}{r}-rBv^{(1)}I[v^{(2)}]-rBv^{(2)}I[v^{(1)}]\Big)d\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{(1)}I[f^{(2)}]d\theta.

Moreover, notice that
f^{(2)}
is a radial function times
v^{(1)}
, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{(1)}I[f^{(2)}]d\theta=0,

this complete the proof.

MM

\square

2.4.7.
Linearized Prandtl equations for
(u_{p}^{(3)},v_{p}^{(4)})
,
(\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)})
and their solvabilities

Let

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=u_{e}(r)+u_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Y)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\big[\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)+\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\varepsilon^{3}[\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,r)+u_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\big[\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)+v_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\varepsilon^{4}v_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Z)+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle p^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=p_{e}(r)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\big[\tilde{p}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)+p_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Y)\big]+\varepsilon^{4}p_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Y)+\text{h.o.t.},

with the boundary conditions

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,1)+u_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,0)=0,\ \lim_{Y\rightarrow\infty}\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)=v_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,0)=0,

we obtain the following linearized Prandtl problem for
(u_{p}^{(3)},v_{p}^{(4)})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(u_{e}(1)+u_{p}^{(0)}\big)\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{(3)}+\big(v_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,1)+v_{p}^{(1)}\big)\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(3)}+(u_{p}^{(3)}+\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,1))\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{(0)}\\[5.0pt]
\quad\quad\quad\quad v_{p}^{(4)}\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(0)}-\partial_{YY}u_{p}^{(3)}=f_{3}(\theta,Y)\\[5.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{(3)}+\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(4)}+\partial_{Y}(Yv_{p}^{(3)})=0,\\[5.0pt]
u_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)=u_{p}^{(3)}(\theta+2\pi,Y),\quad v_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Y)=v_{p}^{(4)}(\theta+2\pi,Y),\\[5.0pt]
u_{p}^{(3)}\big|_{Y=0}=-\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}\big|_{r=1},\ \ \lim\limits_{Y\rightarrow\infty}\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)=v_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,0)=0\end{array}\right.

and

\displaystyle\partial_{Y}p_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Y)=g_{3}(\theta,Y),\ p_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,0)=0,

(2.172)

where

\displaystyle f_{3}(\theta,Y)=

\displaystyle-\partial_{\theta}p_{p}^{(3)}+Y\partial_{YY}u_{p}^{(3)}+\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{(3)}+\sum_{k=0}^{1}(-1)^{k}\frac{Y^{k}\partial_{\theta\theta}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(1-k)}}{k!}+2\partial_{\theta}v_{p}^{(2)}-2Y\partial_{\theta}v_{p}^{(1)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{1}(-1)^{k}\frac{Y^{k}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(1-k)}}{k!}-\sum_{i+j=3,1\leq i\leq 2}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}-\sum_{i+j=3}[v_{p}^{(i)}Y\partial_{Y}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}+\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}v_{p}^{(j)}]

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{i+j=3-k,(k,j)\neq(0,3)}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}+\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(j)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\Big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{i+j=3-k}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k+1}\partial_{Y}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}+v_{p}^{(i)}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}(r\partial_{r}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(j)})(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\Big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{i+j=4-k,(k,j)\neq(0,3),(0,0),i\leq 3}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\partial_{Y}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{i+j=3-k}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}v_{p}^{(j)}+\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(j)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\Big),

\displaystyle g_{3}(\theta,Y)=

\displaystyle\partial_{YY}v_{p}^{(3)}+Y\partial_{YY}v_{p}^{(2)}+\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(2)}+\partial_{\theta\theta}v_{p}^{(1)}-\sum_{k=0}^{1}(-1)^{k}\frac{Y^{k}}{k!}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(2-k)}-v_{p}^{(1)}-Y\partial_{Y}p_{p}^{(3)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{i+j=3}[\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\partial_{\theta}v_{p}^{(j)}+v_{p}^{(i)}Y\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(j)}-\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}]-\sum_{i+j=4}v_{p}^{(i)}Y\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(j)}-\sum_{k=0}^{2}\sum_{i+j=4-k}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(j)}

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{i+j=3-k}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\partial_{\theta}v_{p}^{(j)}+\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\partial_{\theta}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(j)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\Big)

\displaystyle+\sum_{k=0}^{3}\sum_{i+j=3-k}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(j)}+\tilde{u}_{p}^{(i)}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(j)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\Big)

\displaystyle-\sum_{k=0}^{1}\sum_{i+j=3-k}\Big(\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k+1}\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(j)}+v_{p}^{(i)}\frac{\partial_{r}^{k}(r\partial_{r}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(j)})(\theta,1)}{k!}Y^{k}\Big)

with
\tilde{u}_{p}^{(0)}=u_{p}^{(0)},\ \tilde{u}^{(0)}_{e}=u_{e}(r),\ \tilde{u}_{p}^{(3)}=u_{p}^{(3)}.

Similarly, let

\displaystyle u^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=u_{e}(r)+\widehat{u}_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Z)+\sum_{i=1}^{2}\varepsilon^{i}\big[\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)+(\widehat{u}_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Z)-\hat{A}_{i\infty})\big]+\varepsilon^{3}\big[\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,r)+\widehat{u}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z)\big]+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle v^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\big[\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)+\widehat{v}_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Z)\big]+\varepsilon^{4}\widehat{v}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Z)+\text{h.o.t.},

\displaystyle p^{\varepsilon}(\theta,r)=p_{e}(r)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon\big[\tilde{p}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)+\widehat{p}_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Z)\big]+\varepsilon^{4}\widehat{p}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Z)+\text{h.o.t.},

with the following boundary conditions

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}(\theta,r_{0})+\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,0)=0,\ \lim_{Z\rightarrow+\infty}\partial_{Z}\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z)=\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,0)=0,

we obtain the following linearized Prandlt equations for
(\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)})

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\big(u_{e}(r_{0})+\hat{u}_{p}^{(0)}\big)\partial_{\theta}\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}+\big(v_{e}^{(1)}(\theta,r_{0})+\hat{v}_{p}^{(1)}\big)r_{0}\partial_{Z}\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}+\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}\partial_{\theta}\hat{u}_{p}^{(0)}\\[5.0pt]
\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad\quad+\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)}r_{0}\partial_{Z}\hat{u}_{p}^{(0)}-r_{0}\partial_{ZZ}\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}=\hat{f}_{3}(\theta,Z),\\[5.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}+r_{0}\partial_{Z}\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)}+\partial_{Z}(Z\hat{v}_{p}^{(3)})=0,\\[5.0pt]
\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z)=\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta+2\pi,Z),\ \hat{v}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Z)=\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta+2\pi,Z),\\[5.0pt]
\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}\big|_{Z=0}=-\tilde{u}_{e}^{(3)}\big|_{r=r_{0}},\ \lim\limits_{Z\rightarrow+\infty}\partial_{Z}\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z)=\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,0)=0\end{array}\right.

and the pressure
\hat{p}_{p}^{(4)}
satisfies

\displaystyle\partial_{Z}\hat{p}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Z)=\hat{g}_{3}(\theta,Z),\ \hat{p}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,0)=0.

(2.179)

The functions
(\hat{f}_{3}(\theta,Z),\hat{g}_{3}(\theta,Z))
are similar to
(f_{3}(\theta,Y),g_{3}(\theta,Y))
, we omit the details here.

Proposition 2.11
.

There exists
\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, the equations (
2.4.7
) have a unique solution
(u_{p}^{(3)},v_{p}^{(4)})
and the equations (
2.4.7
) have a unique solution
(\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)})
which satisfy

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\sum_{0<j+k\leq m}\int_{-\infty}^{0}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{Y}^{k}(u_{p}^{(3)},v_{p}^{(4)})\big|^{2}\big<Y\big>^{2l}d\theta dY\leq C(m,l),\ \forall\ m,l\geq 0,\\
&\sum_{0<j+k\leq m}\int_{0}^{+\infty}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{Z}^{k}(\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)})\big|^{2}\big<Z\big>^{2l}d\theta dZ\leq C(m,l),\ \forall\ m,l\geq 0,\\
&\int_{0}^{2\pi}v_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Y)d\theta=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Z)d\theta=0,\ \forall\ Y\leq 0,\ Z\geq 0\end{aligned}

(2.180)

and

\displaystyle\|(u_{p}^{(3)},v_{p}^{(4)})\|_{\infty}+\|(\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)})\|_{\infty}\leq C.

The proof is same with Proposition
2.6
, we omit the details. Moreover, we can construct
(p_{p}^{(4)},\hat{p}_{p}^{(4)})
by solving the equations (
2.172
) and (
2.179
).

2.5.
Approximate solutions

In this subsection, we construct an approximate solution of Navier-Stokes equations (
1
).
Set

\displaystyle\tilde{u}_{p}^{a}(\theta,r):=

\displaystyle\chi(r)\Big(u_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Y)+\varepsilon\tilde{u}_{p}^{(1)}(\theta,Y)+\varepsilon^{2}\tilde{u}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Y)+\varepsilon^{3}u_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Y)\Big)

\displaystyle+(1-\chi(r))\Big(\hat{u}_{p}^{(0)}(\theta,Z)+\varepsilon\big[\hat{u}_{p}^{(1)}(\theta,Z)-\hat{A}_{1\infty}\big]+\varepsilon^{2}[\hat{u}_{p}^{(2)}(\theta,Z)-\hat{A}_{2\infty}]+\varepsilon^{3}\hat{u}_{p}^{(3)}(\theta,Z)\Big)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\chi(r)u_{p}^{a}+(1-\chi(r))\hat{u}_{p}^{a},

\displaystyle\tilde{v}_{p}^{a}(\theta,r):=

\displaystyle\chi(r)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\varepsilon^{i}v_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Y)\Big)+(1-\chi(r))\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\varepsilon^{i}\hat{v}_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Z)\Big)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\chi(r)v_{p}^{a}+(1-\chi(r))\hat{v}_{p}^{a},

\displaystyle\tilde{p}_{p}^{a}(\theta,r):=

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(r)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\varepsilon^{i}p_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Y)\Big)+(1-\chi(r))^{2}\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\varepsilon^{i}\hat{p}_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Z)\Big)

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\chi^{2}(r)p_{p}^{a}+(1-\chi(r))^{2}\hat{p}_{p}^{a},

and

\displaystyle u_{e}^{a}(\theta,r):=

\displaystyle u_{e}(r)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r),

\displaystyle v_{e}^{a}(\theta,r):=

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\tilde{v}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r),

\displaystyle p_{e}^{a}(\theta,r):=

\displaystyle p_{e}(r)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\tilde{p}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r).

We construct an approximate solution
(u^{a},v^{a},p^{a})

\displaystyle u^{a}(\theta,r):

\displaystyle=u_{e}^{a}(\theta,r)+\tilde{u}_{p}^{a}(\theta,r)+\varepsilon^{4}h(\theta,r),

(2.181)

\displaystyle v^{a}(\theta,r):

\displaystyle=v_{e}^{a}(\theta,r)+\tilde{v}_{p}^{a}(\theta,r),

(2.182)

\displaystyle p^{a}(\theta,r):

\displaystyle=p_{e}^{a}(\theta,r)+\tilde{p}_{p}^{a}(\theta,r),

(2.183)

where the corrector
h(\theta,r)
will be given in Appendix B which satisfies

\displaystyle h(\theta,1)=0,\ h(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ \|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k}h\|_{2}\leq C(j,k)\varepsilon^{-k}

and makes
(u^{a},v^{a})
be divergence-free

\displaystyle u^{a}_{\theta}+(rv^{a})_{r}=0.

Moreover,
(u^{a},v^{a})
satisfies the following boundary conditions

\displaystyle u^{a}(\theta+2\pi,r)

\displaystyle=u^{a}(\theta,r),\ v^{a}(\theta+2\pi,r)=v^{a}(\theta,r),

\displaystyle u^{a}(\theta,1)

\displaystyle=\alpha+\eta f(\theta),\ v^{a}(\theta,1)=0,

\displaystyle u^{a}(\theta,r_{0})

\displaystyle=\beta+\eta g(\theta),\ v^{a}(\theta,r_{0})=0.

By collecting the estimates (
2.137
), (
2.159
), (
2.164
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\|u^{a}_{e}-u_{e}(r)\|_{\infty}+\|(\partial_{r}(u^{a}_{e}-u_{e}(r)),\partial_{\theta}u^{a}_{e})\|_{\infty}\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon),

\displaystyle\|v^{a}_{e}\|_{\infty}+\|(\partial_{r}v^{a}_{e},\partial_{\theta}v^{a}_{e})\|_{\infty}\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon),

(2.184)

and collecting the estimates (
2.88
), (
2.99
), (
2.157
), (
2.180
) one has

\displaystyle\|(Y^{j}\partial_{Y}^{k}u_{p}^{a},Y^{j}\partial_{\theta}^{k}u_{p}^{a})\|_{\infty}\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon),\ \|(Y^{j}\partial_{Y}^{k}v_{p}^{a},Y^{j}\partial_{\theta}^{k}v_{p}^{a})\|_{\infty}\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon),\ \forall j\leq 2,k\leq 2.

(2.185)

Similarly, there holds

\displaystyle\|(Z^{j}\partial_{Z}^{k}\hat{u}_{p}^{a},Z^{j}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\hat{u}_{p}^{a})\|_{\infty}\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon),\ \|(Z^{j}\partial_{Z}^{k}\hat{v}_{p}^{a},Z^{j}\partial_{\theta}^{k}\hat{v}_{p}^{a})\|_{\infty}\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon),\ \forall j\leq 2,k\leq 2.

By (
1.11
), (
2.181
), (
2.5
) and (
2.185
), we deduce that there exists
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, there holds

\displaystyle u^{a}(\theta,r)\geq d_{0}>0,\ \forall(\theta,r)\in\Omega,

(2.186)

where
d_{0}
is a fixed number.

Finally, set

\displaystyle R_{u}^{a}:

\displaystyle=u^{a}u^{a}_{\theta}+v^{a}ru^{a}_{r}+u^{a}v^{a}+p^{a}_{\theta}-\varepsilon^{2}\Big(ru^{a}_{rr}+\frac{u^{a}_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v^{a}_{\theta}}{r}+u^{a}_{r}-\frac{u^{a}}{r}\Big)-\varepsilon^{2}rF_{u},

\displaystyle R_{v}^{a}:

\displaystyle=u^{a}v^{a}_{\theta}+v^{a}rv^{a}_{r}-(u^{a})^{2}+rp^{a}_{r}-\varepsilon^{2}\Big(rv^{a}_{rr}+\frac{v^{a}_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u^{a}_{\theta}}{r}+v^{a}_{r}-\frac{v^{a}}{r}\Big)-\varepsilon^{2}rF_{v},

then there holds

\displaystyle\|R_{u}^{a}\|_{2}+\|\partial_{\theta}R_{u}^{a}\|_{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{4},\ \|R_{v}^{a}\|_{2}+\|\partial_{\theta}R_{v}^{a}\|_{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{4}

and
(u^{a},v^{a},p^{a})
satisfies

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}u^{a}u^{a}_{\theta}+v^{a}ru^{a}_{r}+u^{a}v^{a}+p^{a}_{\theta}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(ru^{a}_{rr}+\frac{u^{a}_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v^{a}_{\theta}}{r}+u^{a}_{r}-\frac{u^{a}}{r}\big)=\varepsilon^{2}rF_{u}+R_{u}^{a},\ (\theta,r)\in\Omega,\\[5.0pt]
u^{a}v^{a}_{\theta}+v^{a}rv^{a}_{r}-(u^{a})^{2}+rp^{a}_{r}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(rv^{a}_{rr}+\frac{v^{a}_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u^{a}_{\theta}}{r}+v^{a}_{r}-\frac{v^{a}}{r}\big)=\varepsilon^{2}rF_{v}+R_{v}^{a},\ (\theta,r)\in\Omega,\\[5.0pt]
u^{a}_{\theta}+(rv^{a})_{r}=0,\ (\theta,r)\in\Omega,\\[5.0pt]
u^{a}(\theta+2\pi,r)=u^{a}(\theta,r),\ v^{a}(\theta+2\pi,r)=v^{a}(\theta,r),\ (\theta,r)\in\Omega,\\[5.0pt]
u^{a}(\theta,1)=\alpha+f(\theta)\eta,\ v^{a}(\theta,1)=0,\ \theta\in[0,2\pi],\\[5.0pt]
u^{a}(\theta,r_{0})=\beta+g(\theta)\eta,\ v^{a}(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ \theta\in[0,2\pi].\end{array}\right.

3.
Linear stability estimates of error equations

In this section, we derive the error equations and establish the linear stability estimate of the error equations.

3.1.
Error equations

Set the error

\displaystyle u:=u^{\varepsilon}-u^{a},\ v:=v^{\varepsilon}-v^{a},\ p:=p^{\varepsilon}-p^{a},

then there hold

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}u^{a}u_{\theta}+uu^{a}_{\theta}+uu_{\theta}+v^{a}ru_{r}+vru^{a}_{r}+vru_{r}+v^{a}u+vu^{a}+vu+p_{\theta}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)=R_{u}^{a},\\[5.0pt]
u^{a}v_{\theta}+uv^{a}_{\theta}+uv_{\theta}+v^{a}rv_{r}+vrv^{a}_{r}+vrv_{r}-(u^{2}+2uu^{a})+rp_{r}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)=R_{v}^{a},\\[5.0pt]
u_{\theta}+(rv)_{r}=0,\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta+2\pi,r)=u(\theta,r),\ v(\theta+2\pi,r)=v(\theta,r),\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,1)=0,\ v(\theta,1)=0,\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ v(\theta,r_{0})=0.\end{array}\right.

We set

\displaystyle S_{u}:

\displaystyle=u^{a}u_{\theta}+v^{a}ru_{r}+uu^{a}_{\theta}+vru^{a}_{r}+v^{a}u+vu^{a},

\displaystyle S_{v}:

\displaystyle=u^{a}v_{\theta}+v^{a}rv_{r}+uv^{a}_{\theta}+vrv^{a}_{r}-2uu^{a},

then the error equations become

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)+p_{\theta}+S_{u}=R_{u},\\[5.0pt]
-\varepsilon^{2}\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)+rp_{r}+S_{v}=R_{v},\\[5.0pt]
u_{\theta}+(rv)_{r}=0,\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,r)=u(\theta+2\pi,r),\ v(\theta,r)=v(\theta+2\pi,r),\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,1)=0,\ v(\theta,1)=0,\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ v(\theta,r_{0})=0,\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle R_{u}:=R_{u}^{a}-uu_{\theta}-vru_{r}-vu,\ R_{v}:=R_{v}^{a}-uv_{\theta}-vrv_{r}+u^{2}.

3.2.
Linear stability estimate

In this part, we consider the following linear system in
\Omega

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)+p_{\theta}+S_{u}=F_{1},\\[5.0pt]
-\varepsilon^{2}\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)+rp_{r}+S_{v}=F_{2},\\[5.0pt]
u_{\theta}+(rv)_{r}=0,\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,r)=u(\theta+2\pi,r),\ v(\theta,r)=v(\theta+2\pi,r),\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,1)=0,\ v(\theta,1)=0,\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ v(\theta,r_{0})=0,\end{array}\right.

and establish its linear stability estimate (
3.20
).

Firstly, due to the third equation in (
3.2
) and the boundary condition of
v
, we easily deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}v(\theta,r)d\theta=0,\ \forall\ r\in[r_{0},1].

Hence, there holds

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{2}(\theta,r)d\theta\leq\int_{0}^{2\pi}v_{\theta}^{2}(\theta,r)d\theta,\ \forall\ r\in[r_{0},1].

The following stability estimate is a direct result of the basic energy estimate (
3.21
) and positivity estimate (
3.26
).

Proposition 3.1
.

Let
(u,v)
be a smooth solution of (
3.2
), then there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, there holds

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}+\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\|(F_{1},F_{2})\|_{2}^{2}+C\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(uF_{1}+vF_{2}\big)d\theta dr\Big|.

(3.20)

3.2.1.
Basic energy estimate

In this subsection, we establish the following basic energy estimate.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
(u,v)
be a smooth solution of (
3.2
), then there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, there holds

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+C\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[uF_{1}+vF_{2}\big]d\theta dr\Big|.

(3.21)

Proof.

Multiplying the first equation in (
3.1
) by
u
, the second equation in (
3.1
) by
v
, adding them together and integrating in
\Omega
, we obtain that

\displaystyle\underbrace{-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)u+\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)v\Big]d\theta dr}_{diffusion\hskip 8.19447ptterm}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(p_{\theta}u+p_{r}rv)d\theta dr}_{pressure\hskip 8.19447ptterm}+\underbrace{\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(uS_{u}+vS_{v}\big)d\theta dr}_{linear\hskip 8.19447ptterm}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[uF_{u}+vF_{v}\big]d\theta dr.

Next we deal with them term by term.

The diffusion term:
Integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)u+\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)v\Big]d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\bigg(\frac{u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2}}{r}+\frac{u^{2}+v^{2}}{r}+2\frac{u_{\theta}v-uv_{\theta}}{r}\bigg)d\theta dr+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r\big(u_{r}^{2}+v_{r}^{2}\big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{(u_{\theta}+v)^{2}+(v_{\theta}-u)^{2}}{r}d\theta dr+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r\big(u_{r}^{2}+v_{r}^{2}\big)d\theta dr\geq r_{0}\varepsilon^{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}.

(3.22)

Pressure term:
Integrating by parts and using the divergence-free condition, we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(p_{\theta}u+p_{r}(rv)\big)d\theta dr=0.

(3.23)

Linear term:
Integrating by parts and using the divergence-free condition, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(uS_{u}+vS_{v}\big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[u^{a}u_{\theta}u+rv^{a}u_{r}u+uu^{a}_{\theta}u+vu^{a}_{r}ru+(v^{a}u+vu^{a})u\big]d\theta dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[u^{a}v_{\theta}v+rv^{a}v_{r}v+uv^{a}_{\theta}v+vv^{a}_{r}rv-2uu^{a}v\big]d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[u^{2}u^{a}_{\theta}+v^{2}rv^{a}_{r}+v^{a}u^{2}+uvv_{\theta}^{a}+uv(ru^{a}_{r}-u^{a})\big]d\theta dr,

(3.24)

where we used

\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(u^{a}u_{\theta}u+rv^{a}u_{r}u)d\theta dr=\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(u^{a}v_{\theta}v+rv^{a}v_{r}v)d\theta dr=0.

Next, we divide into four steps to estimate (
3.24
) term by term.

1)The term
u^{2}u^{a}_{\theta}
:
We divide it into the Euler part and Prandtl part. Notice that
\partial_{\theta}u_{e}=0
,
integrating by parts and using (
2.5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{2}\partial_{\theta}u^{a}_{e}d\theta dr\Big|=\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{2}\partial_{\theta}(u^{a}_{e}-u_{e})d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}2uu_{\theta}(u^{a}_{e}-u_{e})d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}.

Moreover, integrating by parts, using hardy inequality and (
2.185
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{2}\partial_{\theta}u^{a}_{p}d\theta dr\Big|=\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}2uu_{\theta}u^{a}_{p}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2u}{r-1}u_{\theta}Yu^{a}_{p}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}.

Similarly, there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{2}\partial_{\theta}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}d\theta dr\Big|=\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}2uu_{\theta}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2u}{r-r_{0}}u_{\theta}Zu^{a}_{p}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{2}u_{\theta}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}.

2)The term
v^{2}rv^{a}_{r}
and
uvv^{a}_{\theta}
:
Combining (
2.5
) and (
2.185
), we get

\displaystyle\|v^{a}_{r}\|_{\infty}\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon).

Thus

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{2}rv^{a}_{r}d\theta dr\Big|=\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(rv)^{2}\frac{v^{a}_{r}}{r}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u_{\theta}^{2}d\theta dr.

The same argument gives that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}v_{\theta}^{a}uvd\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}.

3)The term
v^{a}u^{2}
:

We decompose
u(\theta,r)=u_{0}(r)+\tilde{u}(\theta,r)
, where
u_{0}(r)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u(r,\theta)d\theta
, and notice that

\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{a}(r,\theta)d\theta=0,\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1]
, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{a}u^{2}d\theta dr\Big|=\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{a}(2u_{0}\tilde{u}+\tilde{u}^{2})d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)(\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}+\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}),

where we used
\|\tilde{u}\|_{2}\leq c\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}
.

4)The term
uv(ru^{a}_{r}-u^{a})
:
Finally, we deal with the remainder two terms which involve
u,v
together

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[uvru^{a}_{r}-uvu^{a}\big]d\theta dr.

We first consider the leading Euler flow
u_{e}(r)
. We decompose
u(\theta,r)=u_{0}(r)+\tilde{u}(\theta,r)
as above, then

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uvru^{\prime}_{e}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(u_{0}+\tilde{u})vru^{\prime}_{e}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\tilde{u}vru^{\prime}_{e}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2},

where we used

\int_{0}^{2\pi}v(\theta,r)d\theta=0,\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1].

Moreover, by (
2.5
), it’s easy to obtain that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uvr\partial_{r}(u_{e}^{a}-u_{e})d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}.

Thus, we obtain that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uvr\partial_{r}u_{e}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}+C\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}.

The same argument gives

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}vuu_{e}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}+C\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}.

For the Prandtl part, using the Hardy inequality, divergence-free condition and (
2.185
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uvr\partial_{r}u_{p}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{urv}{\varepsilon}\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{rvu}{(r-1)^{2}}Y^{2}\partial_{Y}u_{p}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\Big(\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{u^{2}}{(r-1)^{2}}d\theta dr\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{(rv)^{2}}{(r-1)^{2}}d\theta dr\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}.

The same argument gives

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uvr\partial_{r}\hat{u}_{p}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}.

Recalling that
\tilde{u}_{p}^{a}=\chi(r)u_{p}^{a}+(1-\chi(r))\hat{u}^{a}_{p}
and the fast decay of
(u_{p}^{a},\hat{u}^{a}_{p})
away from the boundary, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uvr\partial_{r}\tilde{u}_{p}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|\leq\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}.

Moreover, by the same argument, we can obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uvu_{p}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uv\hat{u}_{p}^{a}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}.

Summing these estimates together, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[uvru^{a}_{r}-vuu^{a}\big]d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)(\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}+\|v_{\theta}\|_{2})\|u_{r}\|_{2}+C\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}.

Finally, summing the above four steps we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(uS_{u}+vS_{v}\big)d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}+C\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}+C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{r_{0}\varepsilon^{2}}{4}\|u_{r}\|_{2}+C\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}.

(3.25)

Based on the estimates (
3.2.1
), (
3.23
) and (
3.2.1
), we obtain (
3.21
).

MM

\square

3.2.2.
Positivity estimate

In this subsection, we establish the following positivity estimate.

Lemma 3.3
.

Let
(u,v)
be a smooth solution of (
3.2
), then there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, there holds

\displaystyle\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|^{2}_{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}+C\|(F_{1},F_{2})\|_{2}^{2}.

(3.26)

Proof.

Multipling the first equation by
-\big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\big)_{r}
and the second equation by
\big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\big)_{\theta}
, integrating in
\Omega
and summing two terms together, we arrive at

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[-\varepsilon^{2}\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)+p_{\theta}+S_{u}\Big]\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}d\theta dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[-\varepsilon^{2}\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)+rp_{r}+S_{v}\Big]\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[-F_{1}\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}+F_{2}\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}\Big]d\theta dr.

(3.27)

Firstly, we choose
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that (
2.186
) holds.

Positivity term:
We first deal with these terms which are related to
S_{u},S_{v}
.
Recall that

\displaystyle S_{u}:

\displaystyle=u^{a}u_{\theta}+v^{a}ru_{r}+uu^{a}_{\theta}+vru^{a}_{r}+v^{a}u+vu^{a},

\displaystyle S_{v}:

\displaystyle=u^{a}v_{\theta}+v^{a}rv_{r}+uv^{a}_{\theta}+vrv^{a}_{r}-2uu^{a},

we first handle the following terms

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(u^{a}u_{\theta}+vru^{a}_{r}+vu^{a}\big)\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}d\theta dr

\displaystyle\quad\quad+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(u^{a}v_{\theta}-2uu^{a}\big)\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}d\theta dr:=I_{1}+I_{2}.

By (
2.5
),(
2.185
) and (
2.186
), we deduce that

\displaystyle I_{2}:=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(rv_{\theta}^{2}-2ruv_{\theta}-\frac{vv_{\theta}ru_{\theta}^{a}}{u^{a}}+\frac{2uvru_{\theta}^{a}}{u^{a}}\Big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(rv_{\theta}^{2}-\frac{vv_{\theta}ru_{\theta}^{a}}{u^{a}}+\frac{2uvru_{\theta}^{a}}{u^{a}}\Big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle(1-C\eta-C\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv_{\theta}^{2}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2uvru_{\theta}^{a}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr,

where we used

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}-2ruv_{\theta}drd\theta=\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}2u_{\theta}rvdrd\theta=-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}2(rv)_{r}rvdrd\theta=0.

Moreover, by the Hardy inequality, (
2.5
), (
2.185
) and (
2.186
), there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2uvru_{\theta}^{a}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2uvrY\partial_{\theta}u_{p}^{a}}{(r-1)u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|+\varepsilon\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2uvrZ\partial_{\theta}\hat{u}_{p}^{a}}{(r-r_{0})u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|+\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2uvr\partial_{\theta}u_{e}^{a}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|rv_{\theta}\|_{2}+\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2uvr\partial_{\theta}u_{e}^{a}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv_{\theta}^{2}d\theta dr+C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2uv\partial_{\theta}u_{e}^{a}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|.

By (
2.5
), it is easy to get

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{2uv\partial_{\theta}u_{e}^{a}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle I_{2}\geq\big(1-C\eta-C\varepsilon\big)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv_{\theta}^{2}d\theta dr-C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}.

For
I_{1}
, we first decompose it

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[u^{a}u_{\theta}+rvu^{a}_{r}+vu^{a}\Big]\Big(-\frac{r}{u^{a}}u_{\theta}+\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}rv\Big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{2}_{\theta}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r^{2}v\frac{u^{a}_{r}}{u^{a}}u_{\theta}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rvu_{\theta}d\theta dr

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{a}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}u_{\theta}rvd\theta dr-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{a}_{r}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}(rv)^{2}d\theta dr

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{u^{a}}{r}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}(rv)^{2}d\theta dr:=\sum_{i=1}^{6}I_{1i}.

Then, integrating by parts and using the divergence-free condition, we deduce that

\displaystyle I_{12}=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r^{2}v\frac{u^{a}_{r}}{u^{a}}u_{\theta}d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r^{2}v\frac{u^{a}_{r}}{u^{a}}(rv)_{r}d\theta dr=\frac{1}{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(\frac{ru^{a}_{r}}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}(rv)^{2}d\theta dr,

\displaystyle I_{13}=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rvu_{\theta}d\theta dr=-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv(rv)_{r}d\theta dr=0,

\displaystyle I_{14}=

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{a}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}u_{\theta}rvd\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{a}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}(rv)_{r}rvd\theta dr=-\frac{1}{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[u^{a}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}\Big]_{r}(rv)^{2}d\theta dr.

Finally, summing the terms
I_{11},\cdots,I_{16}
, we obtain

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{2}_{\theta}d\theta dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{ru^{a}_{r}}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}-\frac{1}{2}\Big[u^{a}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}\Big]_{r}-u^{a}_{r}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}-\frac{u^{a}}{r}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}\Big](rv)^{2}d\theta dr.

Direct computation gives

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big(\frac{ru^{a}_{r}}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}-\frac{1}{2}\Big[u^{a}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}\Big]_{r}-u^{a}_{r}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}-\frac{u^{a}}{r}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big(\frac{ru^{a}_{r}}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}-\Big(u^{a}_{r}+\frac{u^{a}}{r}\Big)\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{u^{a}(u^{a}_{r}+ru^{a}_{rr})-r(u^{a}_{r})^{2}}{(u^{a})^{2}}-\frac{r}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big[\Big(\frac{u^{a}}{r}\Big)^{2}-(u^{a}_{r})^{2}\Big]

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{u^{a}}\Big(ru^{a}_{rr}+u^{a}_{r}-\frac{u^{a}}{r}\Big).

Notice that
u^{a}=u^{a}_{e}+\tilde{u}_{p}^{a}
, there holds

\displaystyle I_{1}=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{2}_{\theta}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u^{a}}\Big(ru^{a}_{rr}+u^{a}_{r}-\frac{u^{a}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{2}_{\theta}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u^{a}}\Big(\partial_{r}u^{a}_{e}+r\partial{rr}u^{a}_{e}-\frac{u^{a}_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u^{a}}\Big(\partial_{r}\tilde{u}^{a}_{p}+r\partial{rr}\tilde{u}^{a}_{p}-\frac{\tilde{u}_{p}^{a}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{2}_{\theta}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u_{e}}\Big(\partial_{r}u^{a}_{e}+r\partial{rr}u^{a}_{e}-\frac{u^{a}_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{u^{a}}-\frac{1}{u_{e}}\Big)\Big(\partial_{r}u^{a}_{e}+r\partial{rr}u^{a}_{e}-\frac{u^{a}_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u^{a}}\Big(\partial_{r}\tilde{u}^{a}_{p}+r\partial{rr}\tilde{u}^{a}_{p}-\frac{\tilde{u}_{p}^{a}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr.

By (
2.5
), (
2.185
) and (
2.186
), we deduce that
|\frac{1}{u^{a}}-\frac{1}{u_{e}}|\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)
, hence

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(\frac{1}{u^{a}}-\frac{1}{u_{e}}\Big)\Big(\partial_{r}u^{a}_{e}+r\partial{rr}u^{a}_{e}-\frac{u^{a}_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|rv\|_{2}^{2}\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}.

Moreover, by the Hardy inequality, (
2.185
) and (
2.186
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{u^{a}}\Big(\partial_{r}u^{a}_{p}+r\partial{rr}u^{a}_{p}-\frac{u_{p}^{a}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[\frac{(r-1)^{2}}{u^{a}}\Big(\partial_{r}u^{a}_{p}+r\partial_{rr}u^{a}_{p}-\frac{u_{p}^{a}}{r}\Big)\frac{(rv)^{2}}{(r-1)^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[\frac{Y^{2}}{u^{a}}\Big(\varepsilon\partial_{Y}u^{a}_{p}+r\partial_{YY}u^{a}_{p}-\varepsilon^{2}\frac{u_{p}^{a}}{r}\Big)\frac{(rv)^{2}}{(r-1)^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}.

Similarly, there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{u^{a}}\Big(\partial_{r}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}+r\partial{rr}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}-\frac{\hat{u}_{p}^{a}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[\frac{(r-r_{0})^{2}}{u^{a}}\Big(\partial_{r}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}+r\partial_{rr}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}-\frac{\hat{u}_{p}^{a}}{r}\Big)\frac{(rv)^{2}}{(r-r_{0})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}.

Recall that
\tilde{u}_{p}^{a}=\chi(r)u_{p}^{a}+(1-\chi(r))\hat{u}^{a}_{p}
and the fast decay of
(u_{p}^{a},\hat{u}^{a}_{p})
away from the boundary, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[\frac{1}{u^{a}}\Big(\partial_{r}\tilde{u}^{a}_{p}+r\partial{rr}\tilde{u}^{a}_{p}-\frac{\tilde{u}_{p}^{a}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}.

Thus, we deduce that

\displaystyle I_{1}\geq

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{2}_{\theta}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u_{e}}\Big(\partial_{r}u^{a}_{e}+r\partial{rr}u^{a}_{e}-\frac{u^{a}_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr-C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{2}_{\theta}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u_{e}}\Big(\partial_{r}u_{e}+r\partial{rr}u_{e}-\frac{u_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr-C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2},

where we used the fact
u_{e}^{a}(\theta,r):=u_{e}(r)+\sum_{i=1}^{3}\varepsilon^{i}\tilde{u}_{e}^{(i)}(\theta,r)
.

Finally, summing the estimates for
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we obtain

\displaystyle-

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(u^{a}u_{\theta}+vru^{a}_{r}+vu^{a}\big)\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(u^{a}v_{\theta}-2uu^{a}\big)\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}d\theta dr

\displaystyle\geq\Big(1-C\eta-C\varepsilon\Big)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r(u^{2}_{\theta}+v^{2}_{\theta})drd\theta

\displaystyle\quad+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u_{e}}\Big(\partial_{r}u_{e}+r\partial{rr}u_{e}-\frac{u_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr-C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}.

Next, we deal with the remainder terms in
S_{u}

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[v^{a}ru_{r}+uu^{a}_{\theta}+v^{a}u\Big]\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[rv^{a}u_{r}+uu^{a}_{\theta}+v^{a}u\Big]\Big(\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}rv-\frac{r}{u^{a}}u_{\theta}\Big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv^{a}u_{r}\Big(\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}rv-\frac{r}{u^{a}}u_{\theta}\Big)d\theta dr-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uu^{a}_{\theta}\Big(\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}rv-\frac{r}{u^{a}}u_{\theta}\Big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{a}u\Big(\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}rv-\frac{r}{u^{a}}u_{\theta}\Big)d\theta dr:=I^{u}_{1}+I^{u}_{2}+I^{u}_{3}

and the remainder terms in
S_{v}

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[v^{a}rv_{r}+uv^{a}_{\theta}+vrv^{a}_{r}\Big]\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[rv^{a}v_{r}+uv^{a}_{\theta}+rv_{r}^{a}v\Big]\Big(\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}-\frac{rvu_{\theta}^{a}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv^{a}v_{r}\Big(\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}-\frac{rvu_{\theta}^{a}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uv^{a}_{\theta}\Big(\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}-\frac{rvu_{\theta}^{a}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv_{r}^{a}v\Big(\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}-\frac{rvu_{\theta}^{a}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)d\theta dr:=I^{v}_{1}+I^{v}_{2}+I^{v}_{3}.

The remainder terms in
S_{u}
:
By (
2.5
), (
2.185
) and (
2.186
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv^{a}u_{r}\frac{ru_{\theta}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv^{a}u_{r}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}rvd\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}}{u^{a}}-\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}ru^{a}_{r}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}+\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}ru^{a}_{r}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|.

Moreover, by the Hardy inequality, (
2.5
), (
2.185
) and (
2.186
), we deduce

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}r\partial_{r}u^{a}_{p}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}r(r-1)\partial_{r}u^{a}_{p}}{(r-1)(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}rY\partial_{Y}u^{a}_{p}}{(r-1)(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}r\partial_{r}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}r(r-r_{0})\partial_{r}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}}{(r-r_{0})(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}r\partial_{r}u^{a}_{e}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2},

thus, there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{r^{2}vv^{a}u_{r}r\partial_{r}u^{a}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}.

Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle|I^{u}_{1}|\leq(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru_{\theta}^{2}drd\theta+C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u_{r}^{2}drd\theta.

The same argument gives

\displaystyle|I^{u}_{3}|\leq(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru_{\theta}^{2}drd\theta+C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u_{r}^{2}drd\theta.

For
I^{u}_{2}
, the same argument as above gives

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uu^{a}_{\theta}\frac{ru_{\theta}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}.

Moreover, direct computation gives

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uu^{a}_{\theta}\Big(\frac{r}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}rvd\theta dr=\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big(\frac{uu^{a}_{\theta}rv}{u^{a}}-\frac{uu^{a}_{\theta}rv\cdot ru^{a}_{r}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)d\theta dr.

The same argument as above gives

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{uu^{a}_{\theta}rv}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}.

By the Hardy inequality, (
2.5
), (
2.185
) and (
2.186
), we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{uu^{a}_{\theta}rv\cdot r\partial_{r}u^{a}_{p}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{u}{r-1}u^{a}_{\theta}\frac{rv}{r-1}\frac{r(r-1)^{2}\partial_{r}u^{a}_{p}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{u}{r-1}u^{a}_{\theta}\frac{rv}{r-1}\frac{rY^{2}\partial_{Y}u^{a}_{p}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{uu^{a}_{\theta}rv\cdot r\partial_{r}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{u}{r-r_{0}}u^{a}_{\theta}\frac{rv}{r-r_{0}}\frac{r(r-r_{0})^{2}\partial_{r}\hat{u}^{a}_{p}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}.

Furthermore, using the above argument and noting the fact
\|\partial_{\theta}u_{e}^{a}\|_{2}\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)
, there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{uu^{a}_{\theta}rv\cdot r\partial_{r}u^{a}_{e}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}.

Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle|I^{u}_{2}|\leq(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v^{2}_{\theta})drd\theta+C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u_{r}^{2}drd\theta.

Thus, there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[v^{a}ru_{r}+uu^{a}_{\theta}+v^{a}u\Big]\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v^{2}_{\theta})drd\theta+C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u_{r}^{2}drd\theta.

(3.28)

The remainder terms in
S_{v}
:

Using
v^{a}=O(\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon))
, we deduce that

\displaystyle|I_{1}^{v}|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2},\ |I_{2}^{v}|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}.

For
I^{v}_{3}
, using the Hardy inequality, (
2.5
), (
2.185
) and (
2.186
), we deduce

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r\partial_{r}v_{e}^{a}v\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r\partial_{r}v_{p}^{a}v\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(r-1)\partial_{r}v_{p}^{a}\frac{rv}{r-1}\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}Y\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{a}\frac{rv}{r-1}\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r\partial_{r}\hat{v}_{p}^{a}v\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}(r-r_{0})\partial_{r}v_{p}^{a}\frac{rv}{r-r_{0}}\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2},

hence there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv_{r}^{a}v\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}.

The same argument gives

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv_{r}^{a}v\frac{rvu_{\theta}^{a}}{(u^{a})^{2}}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}.

Collecting these estimates, we get

\displaystyle|I_{3}^{v}|\leq C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}(\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}+\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}).

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[v^{a}rv_{r}+uv^{a}_{\theta}+vrv^{a}_{r}\Big]\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq
