\displaystyle C(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v^{2}_{\theta})drd\theta+C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u_{r}^{2}drd\theta.

(3.29)

Finally, collecting (
3.28
) and (
3.29
), we can choose
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, there holds

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}S_{u}\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}S_{v}\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}d\theta dr

\displaystyle\quad\quad\geq

\displaystyle\Big(1-C\eta-C\varepsilon\Big)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r(u^{2}_{\theta}+v^{2}_{\theta})drd\theta

(3.30)

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u_{e}}\Big(\partial_{r}u_{e}+r\partial{rr}u_{e}-\frac{u_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr-C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u_{r}^{2}drd\theta.

Pressure estimate:
Integrating by parts, we deduce that

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}p_{\theta}\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rp_{r}\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}p_{r\theta}\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}d\theta dr-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}p_{r\theta}\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}d\theta dr=0.

(3.31)

Diffusion term:

Finally, we deal with the diffusion term:

\displaystyle-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[-\varepsilon^{2}\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)\Big]\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}d\theta dr

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[-\varepsilon^{2}\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)\Big]\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(\frac{1}{r}u_{\theta\theta}+ru_{rr}+u_{r}+\frac{2}{r}v_{\theta}-\frac{1}{r}u\Big)\frac{(r^{2}v)_{r}}{u^{a}}drd\theta

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(\frac{1}{r}u_{\theta\theta}+ru_{rr}+u_{r}+\frac{2}{r}v_{\theta}-\frac{1}{r}u\Big)\frac{r^{2}vu_{r}^{a}}{(u^{a})^{2}}drd\theta

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(\frac{1}{r}v_{\theta\theta}+rv_{rr}+v_{r}-\frac{2}{r}u_{\theta}-\frac{1}{r}v\Big)\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}drd\theta

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(\frac{1}{r}v_{\theta\theta}+rv_{rr}+v_{r}-\frac{2}{r}u_{\theta}-\frac{1}{r}v\Big)\frac{rvu_{\theta}^{a}}{(u^{a})^{2}}drd\theta:=\sum_{i=1}^{4}I_{i}.

(3.32)

We first compute
I_{1}
term by term. By the divergence-free condition and (
2.186
), we have

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\frac{1}{r}u_{\theta\theta}\frac{(r^{2}v)_{r}}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\frac{1}{r}u_{\theta\theta}\frac{r(rv)_{r}+rv}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}u_{\theta\theta}\frac{-u_{\theta}+v}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big[u_{\theta}^{2}\Big(\frac{1}{2u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}-u_{\theta}\Big(\frac{v_{\theta}}{u^{a}}-\frac{vu^{a}_{\theta}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)\Big]drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta

and

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}ru_{rr}\frac{(r^{2}v)_{r}}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}ru_{rr}\frac{r(rv)_{r}+rv}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\frac{-r^{2}u_{rr}u_{\theta}+r^{2}u_{rr}v}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big[u_{r}\Big(\frac{r^{2}u_{\theta}}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}-u_{r}\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}\Big]drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big[u_{r}\Big(\frac{r^{2}u_{r\theta}+2ru_{\theta}}{u^{a}}-\frac{r^{2}u_{\theta}u^{a}_{r}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)

\displaystyle-u_{r}\Big(\frac{rv+r(rv)_{r}}{u^{a}}-\frac{r^{2}vu^{a}_{r}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)\Big]drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\sqrt{r}u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+C\eta\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}+\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\frac{r^{2}u_{r\theta}u_{r}}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\eta}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|\sqrt{r}u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+C\eta\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}.

The other low order terms can be easily estimated as follows

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(u_{r}+\frac{2}{r}v_{\theta}-\frac{1}{r}u\Big)\frac{(r^{2}v)_{r}}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta+C\varepsilon\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}u_{r}^{2}drd\theta.

Thus,

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta+C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}u_{r}^{2}drd\theta.

By the same argument as above, we can deduce the following estimate for
I_{2}
:

\displaystyle|I_{2}|

\displaystyle=\varepsilon^{2}\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(\frac{1}{r}u_{\theta\theta}+ru_{rr}+u_{r}+\frac{2}{r}v_{\theta}-\frac{1}{r}u\Big)\frac{r^{2}v\partial_{r}u^{a}}{(u^{a})^{2}}drd\theta\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta+C\varepsilon^{2}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}u_{r}^{2}drd\theta.

Summing the estiamtes of
I_{1}
and
I_{2}
, we obtain that

\displaystyle|I_{1}|+|I_{2}|\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|\sqrt{r}(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}.

For
I_{3}
, the high order terms can be estimated by integrating by parts:

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(\frac{1}{r}v_{\theta\theta}+rv_{rr}\Big)\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|\leq\frac{C}{\varepsilon}\|\sqrt{r}(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

The lower order terms can be estimated by the divergence-free condition and (
2.186
)

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(v_{r}-\frac{2}{r}u_{\theta}-\frac{1}{r}v\Big)\frac{r\partial_{\theta}v}{u^{a}}drd\theta\Big|\leq C\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle|I_{3}|\leq C\varepsilon\|\sqrt{r}(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

For
I_{4}
, the same argument as above gives that

\displaystyle|I_{4}|\leq C\varepsilon^{2}\|\sqrt{r}(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}..

Finally, we can choose
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, there holds

\displaystyle|I_{3}|+|I_{4}|\leq C\varepsilon\|\sqrt{r}(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

Hence, we deduce that there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, the diffusive term (
3.32
) can be controlled by

\displaystyle C(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta+C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}

which combining with the estimates (
3.2.2
) and (
3.2.2
) implies that there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, the lift hand side of
(\ref{p:positivity estimate})
has a lower bound

\displaystyle\Big(1-C\eta-C\varepsilon\Big)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u_{e}}\Big(\partial_{r}u_{e}+r\partial{rr}u_{e}-\frac{u_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr-C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}.

(3.33)

Let
g=\frac{rv}{u_{e}}
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta=\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(r[(u_{e}g)_{r}]^{2}+\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g_{\theta}^{2}\Big)drd\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(ru^{2}_{e}g_{r}^{2}+\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g_{\theta}^{2}\Big)drd\theta-\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}u_{e}\Big(\partial_{r}u_{e}+r\partial{rr}u_{e}\Big)g^{2}drd\theta.

Thus,

\displaystyle\Big(1-C\eta-C\varepsilon\Big)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{1}{u_{e}}\Big(\partial_{r}u_{e}+r\partial{rr}u_{e}-\frac{u_{e}}{r}\Big)(rv)^{2}d\theta dr

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(ru^{2}_{e}g_{r}^{2}+\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g_{\theta}^{2}-\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g^{2}\Big)drd\theta-C(\eta+\varepsilon)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta.

By the Poincaré inequality,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g_{\theta}^{2}drd\theta

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g^{2}drd\theta,

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}ru^{2}_{e}g_{r}^{2}drd\theta

\displaystyle\geq\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g^{2}drd\theta,

here we have used the fact that
u_{e}
has a positive lower bound and upper bound in the second inequality.

Thus

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(ru^{2}_{e}g_{r}^{2}+\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g_{\theta}^{2}-\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g^{2}\Big)drd\theta

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(\frac{1}{2}ru^{2}_{e}g_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{2C}\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g^{2}+\frac{1}{2C}\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g_{\theta}^{2}+\Big(1-\frac{1}{2C}\Big)\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g-\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g^{2}\Big)drd\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\Big(\frac{1}{2}ru^{2}_{e}g_{r}^{2}+\frac{1}{2C}\frac{u_{e}^{2}}{r}g_{\theta}^{2}\Big)drd\theta

\displaystyle\geq

\displaystyle\widetilde{C}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta,

where we used

\displaystyle|u_{\theta}|^{2}=|(rv)_{r}|^{2}=|(gu_{e})_{r}|^{2}\leq C(g^{2}+g^{2}_{r}).

So (
3.2.2
) has a lower bound

\displaystyle\Big(\widetilde{C}-C\eta-C\varepsilon\Big)\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta-C\varepsilon^{2}(\eta+\varepsilon)\|u_{r}\|_{2}^{2}.

(3.34)

Thanks to the Young inequality and Poincaré inequality, the right hand side of
(\ref{p:positivity estimate})
can be directly estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[F_{1}\Big(\frac{r^{2}v}{u^{a}}\Big)_{r}+F_{2}\Big(\frac{rv}{u^{a}}\Big)_{\theta}\Big]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[F_{1}\Big(\frac{r(rv)_{r}+rv}{u^{a}}-\frac{r^{2}vu^{a}_{r}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)+F_{2}\Big(\frac{rv_{\theta}}{u^{a}}-\frac{rvu^{a}_{\theta}}{(u^{a})^{2}}\Big)\Big]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\widetilde{C}}{4}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}r(u_{\theta}^{2}+v_{\theta}^{2})drd\theta+C\|(F_{1},F_{2})\|_{2}^{2}.

(3.35)

Finally, collecting the estimate (
3.34
) and (
3.2.2
) together, we obtain (
3.26
), this complete the proof of this lemma.

MM

\square

4.
Existence of error equations

To deal with the nonlinear term and close the estimate, we need to estimate
\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}
. To this end, we consider the following “
H^{2}
estimate” for a Stokes system.

4.1.
“
H^{2}
estimate” for Stokes system

We consider the Stoke equations

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)+p_{\theta}=f_{u},\\[5.0pt]
-\varepsilon^{2}\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)+rp_{r}=g_{v},\\[5.0pt]
u_{\theta}+\partial_{r}(rv)=0,\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,r)=u(\theta+2\pi,r),\ v(\theta,r)=v(\theta+2\pi,r),\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,1)=0,\ v(\theta,1)=0,\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ v(\theta,r_{0})=0.\end{array}\right.

Lemma 4.1
.

Let
(u,v)
be a smooth solution of (
4.1
), then we have

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[(u_{\theta\theta}+v_{\theta})^{2}+(v_{\theta\theta}-u_{\theta})^{2}]d\theta dr+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(u_{r\theta}^{2}+v_{r\theta}^{2}\big)d\theta dr

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[f_{u}u_{\theta\theta}+g_{v}v_{\theta\theta}\big]drd\theta\Big|.

(4.7)

Proof.

Multipling
u_{\theta\theta}
for the first equation and
v_{\theta\theta}
for the second equation in (
4.1
), integrating in
\Omega
and summing two terms together, we arrive at

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[-\varepsilon^{2}\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)+p_{\theta}\Big]u_{\theta\theta}drd\theta

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[-\varepsilon^{2}\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)+rp_{r}\Big]v_{\theta\theta}drd\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[f_{u}u_{\theta\theta}+g_{v}v_{\theta\theta}\big]drd\theta.

Integrating by parts and using the divergence-free condition, we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big[p_{\theta}u_{\theta\theta}+p_{r}rv_{\theta\theta}\big]drd\theta=0.

Then, we compute the diffusive term. Integrating by parts, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}-\varepsilon^{2}u_{rr}ru_{\theta\theta}drd\theta=-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\varepsilon^{2}u_{r\theta}(ru_{\theta})_{r}drd\theta=-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru_{r\theta}^{2}drd\theta,

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}-\varepsilon^{2}v_{rr}rv_{\theta\theta}drd\theta=-\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\varepsilon^{2}v_{r\theta}(rv_{\theta})_{r}drd\theta=-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv_{r\theta}^{2}drd\theta,

and

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\varepsilon^{2}u_{r}u_{\theta\theta}drd\theta=\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\varepsilon^{2}v_{r}v_{\theta\theta}drd\theta=0,

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\varepsilon^{2}\frac{u}{r}u_{\theta\theta}drd\theta+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\varepsilon^{2}\frac{v}{r}v_{\theta\theta}drd\theta=-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{u_{\theta}^{2}+v^{2}_{\theta}}{r}drd\theta.

Summing all terms, we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[-\varepsilon^{2}\big(ru_{rr}+\frac{u_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{v_{\theta}}{r}+u_{r}-\frac{u}{r}\big)+p_{\theta}\Big]u_{\theta\theta}drd\theta

\displaystyle+\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[-\varepsilon^{2}\big(rv_{rr}+\frac{v_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{u_{\theta}}{r}+v_{r}-\frac{v}{r}\big)+rp_{r}\Big]v_{\theta\theta}drd\theta

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\frac{(u_{\theta\theta}+v_{\theta})^{2}+(v_{\theta\theta}-u_{\theta})^{2}}{r}d\theta dr-\varepsilon^{2}\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}r\big(u_{r\theta}^{2}+v_{r\theta}^{2}\big)d\theta dr.

Thus we obtain (
4.1
) and then
complete the proof of this lemma.

MM

\square

4.2.
Anisotropic Sobolev embedding

To obtain the
L^{\infty}
estimate, we need the following anisotropic Sobolev embedding.

Lemma 4.2
.

Let
(u,v)
be smooth function and satisfy
(u,v)|_{r=1}=(u,v)|_{r=r_{0}}=(0,0)
, then

\displaystyle\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}\leq C(\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}+\|(u_{r},v_{r})\|_{2}+\|(u_{r\theta},v_{r\theta})\|_{2}).

(4.8)

Proof.

In fact, let

\displaystyle u(\theta,r)=\sum_{k\in Z}u_{k}(r)e^{ik\theta},\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1],

thus

\displaystyle|u(\theta,r)|\leq\sum_{k\in Z}|u_{k}(r)|,\ \forall(\theta,r)\in[0,2\pi]\times[r_{0},1].

Notice that
u(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ \forall\theta\in[0,2\pi]
gives
u_{k}(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ \forall\theta\in[0,2\pi],k\in Z
, hence

\displaystyle\|u_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{2}\|u_{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}\|u^{\prime}_{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}.

Thus,

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq 0}\|u_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq\sqrt{2}\sum_{k\neq 0}\|u_{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}\|u^{\prime}_{k}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}\leq C\Big(\sum_{k\neq 0}|k|^{2}\|u_{k}\|^{2}_{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}\Big(\sum_{k\neq 0}|k|^{2}\|u^{\prime}_{k}\|^{2}_{2}\Big)^{\frac{1}{4}}.

Moreover, it’s easy to get

\displaystyle u_{\theta}(\theta,r)=\sum_{k\in Z}iku_{k}(r)e^{ik\theta},\quad u_{r\theta}(\theta,r)=\sum_{k\in Z}iku^{\prime}_{k}(r)e^{ik\theta},\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1].

Thus,

\displaystyle\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{k\in Z}|k|^{2}\|u_{k}\|_{2}^{2},\quad\|u_{r\theta}\|_{2}^{2}=\sum_{k\in Z}|k|^{2}\|u^{\prime}_{k}\|_{2}^{2}.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\sum_{k\neq 0}\|u_{k}\|_{\infty}\leq C\|u_{\theta}\|^{\frac{1}{2}}_{2}\|u_{r\theta}\|_{2}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Moreover, due to
u_{0}(r)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u(\theta,r)d\theta
and
u(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ \forall\theta\in[0,2\pi]
, it’s easy to get

\displaystyle|u_{0}(r)|\leq C\|u_{r}\|_{2},\quad\forall r\in[r_{0},1].

Finally, we obtain

\displaystyle\|u\|_{\infty}\leq C(\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}+\|u_{r}\|_{2}+\|u_{r\theta}\|_{2}).

Similarly we can obtain the same result for
v
and hence we complete the proof of this lemma.

MM

\square

4.3.
Existence for the error equations

We apply the contraction mapping theorem to prove the existence for the error equations (
3.1
).

Proposition 4.3
.

There exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, the error equations (
3.1
) have a unique solution
(u,v)
which satisfies

\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}\leq C\varepsilon.

Proof.

For each smooth function
(u,v)
which satisfies

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}u_{\theta}+(rv)_{r}=0,\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,r)=u(\theta+2\pi,r),\ v(\theta,r)=v(\theta+2\pi,r),\\[5.0pt]
u(\theta,1)=0,\ u(\theta,r_{0})=0,\\[5.0pt]
v(\theta,1)=0,\ v(\theta,r_{0})=0,\end{array}\right.

we consider the following linear problem:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(r\bar{u}_{rr}+\frac{\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{\bar{v}_{\theta}}{r}+\bar{u}_{r}-\frac{\bar{u}}{r}\big)+\bar{p}_{\theta}+S_{\bar{u}}=R_{u},\\[7.0pt]
-\varepsilon^{2}\big(r\bar{v}_{rr}+\frac{\bar{v}_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{\bar{u}_{\theta}}{r}+\bar{v}_{r}-\frac{\bar{v}}{r}\big)+r\bar{p}_{r}+S_{\bar{v}}=R_{v},\\[7.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}\bar{u}+\partial_{r}(r\bar{v})=0,\\[7.0pt]
\bar{u}(\theta,r)=\bar{u}(\theta+2\pi,r),\ \bar{v}(\theta,r)=\bar{v}(\theta+2\pi,r),\\[7.0pt]
\bar{u}(\theta,1)=0,\ \bar{v}(\theta,1)=0,\\[7.0pt]
\bar{u}(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ \bar{v}(\theta,r_{0})=0,\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle S_{\bar{u}}:=

\displaystyle u^{a}\bar{u}_{\theta}+v^{a}r\bar{u}_{r}+\bar{u}u^{a}_{\theta}+\bar{v}ru^{a}_{r}+v^{a}\bar{u}+\bar{v}u^{a},

\displaystyle S_{\bar{v}}:=

\displaystyle u^{a}\bar{v}_{\theta}+v^{a}r\bar{v}_{r}+\bar{u}v^{a}_{\theta}+\bar{v}rv^{a}_{r}-2\bar{u}u^{a},

\displaystyle R_{u}:=

\displaystyle R_{u}^{a}-uu_{\theta}-vru_{r}-vu,\quad R_{v}:=R_{v}^{a}-uv_{\theta}-vrv_{r}-u^{2}.

By linear stability estimate (
3.20
), we deduce that there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, there holds

\displaystyle\|(\bar{u}_{\theta},\bar{v}_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+\varepsilon^{2}\|\bar{u}_{r}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\|(R_{u},R_{v})\|_{2}^{2}+\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\big(R_{u}\bar{u}+R_{v}\bar{v}\big)d\theta dr\Big|.

Direct computation gives that

\displaystyle\|(R_{u},R_{v})\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\|(R^{a}_{u},R^{a}_{v})\|_{2}^{2}+C\|(u,v)\|^{2}_{\infty}\|(u_{r},u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

Moreover, there hold

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}R_{u}\bar{u}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[R^{a}_{u}\bar{u}-uu_{\theta}\bar{u}-rvu_{r}\bar{u}-vu\bar{u}]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\bar{u}_{r}\|_{2}\|R^{a}_{u}\|_{2}+C\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u\|_{2}\|u\|_{\infty}+C\|\bar{u}_{r}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u\|_{\infty},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}R_{v}\bar{v}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[R^{a}_{v}\bar{v}-uv_{\theta}\bar{v}-rvv_{r}\bar{v}-u^{2}\bar{v}]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|R^{a}_{v}\|_{2}+C\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u\|_{\infty}+C\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}\|v\|_{\infty}

\displaystyle+C\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|u_{r}\|_{2}\|u\|_{\infty}.

Hence,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[\bar{u}R_{u}+\bar{v}R_{v}]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|(\bar{u}_{\theta},\bar{v}_{\theta},\varepsilon\bar{u}_{r})\|_{2}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-2}\|(R^{a}_{u},R_{v}^{a})\|^{2}_{2}+C\varepsilon^{-2}\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta},\varepsilon u_{r})\|_{2}^{2}\|(u,v)\|^{2}_{\infty}.

Thus, there holds

\displaystyle\|(\bar{u}_{\theta},\bar{v}_{\theta},\varepsilon\bar{u}_{r}\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\|(R^{a}_{u},R^{a}_{v})\|_{2}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-2}\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta},\varepsilon u_{r})\|_{2}^{2}\|(u,v)\|^{2}_{\infty}.

(4.19)

Then, we consider the following Stokes problem:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(r\bar{u}_{rr}+\frac{\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{\bar{v}_{\theta}}{r}+\bar{u}_{r}-\frac{\bar{u}}{r}\big)+\bar{p}_{\theta}=R_{u}-S_{\bar{u}},\\[7.0pt]
-\varepsilon^{2}\big(r\bar{v}_{rr}+\frac{\bar{v}_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{\bar{u}_{\theta}}{r}+\bar{v}_{r}-\frac{\bar{v}}{r}\big)+r\bar{p}_{r}=R_{v}-S_{\bar{v}},\\[7.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}\bar{u}+\partial_{r}(r\bar{v})=0,\\[7.0pt]
\bar{u}(\theta,r)=\bar{u}(\theta+2\pi,r),\ \bar{v}(\theta,r)=\bar{v}(\theta+2\pi,r),\\[7.0pt]
\bar{u}(\theta,1)=0,\ \bar{v}(\theta,1)=0,\\[7.0pt]
\bar{u}(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ \bar{v}(\theta,r_{0})=0.\end{array}\right.

By the Stoke estimate (
4.1
), we deduce

\displaystyle\|(\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}+\bar{v}_{\theta},\bar{v}_{\theta\theta}-\bar{u}_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+\|(\bar{u}_{r\theta},\bar{v}_{r\theta})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}\Big[(R_{u}-S_{\bar{u}})\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}+(R_{v}-S_{\bar{v}})\bar{v}_{\theta\theta}\Big]drd\theta\Big|.

We compute the right hand term by terms.

1)The term
\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}R_{u}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr
and
\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}R_{v}\bar{v}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr
:
First, there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}R_{u}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle=\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[R^{a}_{u}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}-uu_{\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}-rvu_{r}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}-vu\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|\partial_{\theta}R^{a}_{u}\|_{2}+\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[uu_{\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}+rvu_{r}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}+vu\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}]d\theta dr\Big|.

It is easy to obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[uu_{\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}+vu\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}]d\theta dr\Big|\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}(\|\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}+\bar{v}_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\|\bar{v}_{r\theta}\|_{2}^{2})+\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|u\|_{\infty}^{2}\|u_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}.

Integrating by part, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rvu_{r}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[(rv)_{\theta r}u\bar{u}_{\theta}+(rv)_{\theta}u\bar{u}_{\theta r}+(rv)_{r}u_{\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta}+rvu_{\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta r}]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|\bar{u}_{r\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}^{2}\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[u_{\theta\theta}u\bar{u}_{\theta}+u_{\theta}^{2}\bar{u}_{\theta}]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|\bar{u}_{r\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}^{2}\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}uu_{\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}+\bar{v}_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|(\bar{u}_{r\theta},\bar{v}_{r\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}^{2}\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

Thus, we obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}R_{u}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|\partial_{\theta}R^{a}_{u}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}+\bar{v}_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|(\bar{u}_{r\theta},\bar{v}_{r\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}^{2}\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

Similarly,

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}R_{v}\bar{v}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|\leq

\displaystyle C\|\bar{v}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|\partial_{\theta}R^{a}_{u}\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|\bar{v}_{\theta\theta}-\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|(\bar{u}_{r\theta},\bar{v}_{r\theta})\|_{2}^{2}+\frac{C}{\varepsilon^{2}}\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}^{2}\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

2)The term
\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}S_{\bar{u}}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr
and
\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}S_{\bar{v}}\bar{v}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr
:

First, there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}S_{\bar{u}}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|=\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[u^{a}\bar{u}_{\theta}+v^{a}r\bar{u}_{r}+u^{a}_{\theta}\bar{u}+ru^{a}_{r}\bar{v}+v^{a}\bar{u}+u^{a}\bar{v}]\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|.

Integrating by parts, we deduce that

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{a}\bar{u}_{\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{a}_{\theta}\bar{u}^{2}_{\theta}d\theta dr\Big|\leq C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}rv^{a}\bar{u}_{r}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[(rv^{a})_{\theta}\bar{u}_{r}+rv^{a}\bar{u}_{r\theta}]\bar{u}_{\theta}d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|(\bar{u}_{r},\bar{u}_{r\theta})\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{a}_{\theta}\bar{u}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[u^{a}_{\theta}\bar{u}^{2}_{\theta}+u^{a}_{\theta\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta}\bar{u}]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|^{2}_{2}+C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|\bar{u}_{r}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}ru^{a}_{r}\bar{v}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[(ru_{r}^{a})_{\theta}\bar{v}\bar{u}_{\theta}+ru^{a}_{r}\bar{v}_{\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta}]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\eta+\varepsilon)\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|^{2}_{2}+C\varepsilon^{-1}\eta\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|\bar{v}_{\theta}\|_{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}v^{a}\bar{u}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[v^{a}_{\theta}\bar{u}\bar{u}_{\theta}+v^{a}\bar{u}^{2}_{\theta}]d\theta dr\Big|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|\bar{u}_{r}\|_{2}+C\varepsilon(\eta+\varepsilon)\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2},

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}u^{a}\bar{v}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|=

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}[u^{a}_{\theta}\bar{v}\bar{u}_{\theta}+u^{a}\bar{v}_{\theta}\bar{u}_{\theta}]d\theta dr\Big|\leq C(\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}\|\bar{v}_{\theta}\|_{2}+\|\bar{u}_{\theta}\|_{2}^{2}).

Thus, there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, there holds

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}S_{\bar{u}}\bar{u}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|\bar{u}_{r\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-1}\|(\bar{u}_{\theta},\bar{v}_{\theta},\varepsilon\bar{u}_{r})\|^{2}_{2}.

Similarly, we can obtain

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{r_{0}}^{1}\int_{0}^{2\pi}S_{\bar{v}}\bar{v}_{\theta\theta}d\theta dr\Big|\leq\frac{\varepsilon^{2}}{10C}\|\bar{u}_{r\theta}\|_{2}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-1}\|(\bar{u}_{\theta},\bar{v}_{\theta},\varepsilon\bar{u}_{r})\|^{2}_{2}.

Thus, we deduce that

\displaystyle\|(\bar{u}_{r\theta},\bar{v}_{r\theta})\|_{2}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\|(\partial_{\theta}R_{u}^{a},\partial_{\theta}R_{v}^{a})\|_{2}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-3}\|(\bar{u}_{\theta},\bar{v}_{\theta},\varepsilon\bar{u}_{r})\|^{2}_{2}+C\varepsilon^{-4}\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}^{2}\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta})\|_{2}^{2}.

(4.26)

Finally, combining (
4.8
),(
4.19
) and (
4.26
), we obtain the following
L^{\infty}
estimate: there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, there holds

\displaystyle\|(\bar{u},\bar{v})\|_{\infty}^{2}\leq

\displaystyle C\varepsilon^{-4}\|(R_{u}^{a},R_{v}^{a},\partial_{\theta}R_{u}^{a},\partial_{\theta}R_{v}^{a})\|_{2}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-3}\|(\bar{u}_{\theta},\bar{v}_{\theta},\varepsilon\bar{u}_{r})\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\quad\quad+C\varepsilon^{-4}\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta},\varepsilon u_{r})\|_{2}^{2}\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}^{2}.

(4.27)

Set

\displaystyle\|(u,v)\|_{Y}^{2}:=\|(u_{\theta},v_{\theta},\varepsilon u_{r})\|_{2}^{2}+\kappa\varepsilon^{3}\|(u,v)\|_{\infty}^{2},

where
\kappa\ll 1
is a fixed positive number, and combining the estimates (
4.19
) and (
4.3
), we arrive at

\displaystyle\|(\bar{u},\bar{v})\|_{Y}^{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{-2}\|(R^{a}_{u},R^{a}_{v},\partial_{\theta}R_{u}^{a},\partial_{\theta}R_{v}^{a})\|_{2}^{2}+C\varepsilon^{-5}\|(u,v)\|_{Y}^{4}.

(4.28)

Let
Y=\{(u,v)\in C^{\infty}:(u,v)\ satisfes\ (\ref{iterative conditon})\ and\ \|(u,v)\|_{Y}<+\infty\}
.
Thus, due to

\displaystyle\|(R_{u}^{a},R_{v}^{a},\partial_{\theta}R_{u}^{a},\partial_{\theta}R_{v}^{a})\|_{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{4},

there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, the operator

\displaystyle(u,v)\mapsto(\bar{u},\bar{v})

maps the ball
\{(u,v):\|(u,v)\|^{2}_{Y}\leq 2C^{3}\varepsilon^{6}\}
in
Y
into itself.

Moreover, for every two pairs

(u_{1},v_{1})
and
(u_{2},v_{2})
in the ball, we have

\displaystyle\|(\bar{u}_{1}-\bar{u}_{2},\bar{v}_{1}-\bar{v}_{2})\|^{2}_{Y}\leq C\varepsilon^{-5}(\|(u_{1},v_{1})\|_{Y}^{2}+\|(u_{2},v_{2})\|_{Y}^{2})\|(u_{1}-u_{2},v_{1}-v_{2})\|_{Y}^{2}.

(4.29)

In fact, set

\displaystyle\bar{U}:=\bar{u}_{1}-\bar{u}_{2},\quad\bar{V}:=\bar{v}_{1}-\bar{v}_{2},\quad\bar{P}:=\bar{p}_{1}-\bar{p}_{2},

then we have

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{lll}-\varepsilon^{2}\big(r\bar{U}_{rr}+\frac{\bar{U}_{\theta\theta}}{r}+2\frac{\bar{V}_{\theta}}{r}+\bar{U}_{r}-\frac{\bar{U}}{r}\big)+\bar{P}_{\theta}+S_{\bar{U}}=R_{U},\\[7.0pt]
-\varepsilon^{2}\big(r\bar{V}_{rr}+\frac{\bar{V}_{\theta\theta}}{r}-2\frac{\bar{U}_{\theta}}{r}+\bar{V}_{r}-\frac{\bar{V}}{r}\big)+r\bar{P}_{r}+S_{\bar{V}}=R_{V},\\[7.0pt]
\partial_{\theta}\bar{U}+\partial_{r}(r\bar{V})=0,\\[7.0pt]
\bar{U}(\theta,r)=\bar{U}(\theta+2\pi,r),\ \bar{V}(\theta,r)=\bar{V}(\theta+2\pi,r),\\[7.0pt]
\bar{U}(\theta,1)=0,\ \bar{V}(\theta,1)=0,\\[7.0pt]
\bar{U}(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ \bar{V}(\theta,r_{0})=0,\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle R_{U}\triangleq R_{u_{1}}-R_{u_{2}}=

\displaystyle u_{2}u_{2\theta}+v_{2}ru_{2r}+v_{2}u_{2}-u_{1}u_{1\theta}-v_{1}ru_{1r}-v_{1}u_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(u_{2}-u_{1})\partial_{\theta}u_{2}+u_{1}\partial_{\theta}(u_{2}-u_{1})

\displaystyle+(v_{2}-v_{1})r\partial_{r}u_{2}+v_{1}r\partial_{r}(u_{2}-u_{1})+(v_{2}-v_{1})u_{2}+v_{1}(u_{2}-u_{1}),

\displaystyle R_{V}\triangleq R_{v_{1}}-R_{v_{2}}=

\displaystyle u_{2}v_{2\theta}+v_{2}rv_{2r}-u_{2}^{2}-u_{1}v_{1\theta}-v_{1}rv_{1r}+u^{2}_{1}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle(u_{2}-u_{1})\partial_{\theta}v_{2}+u_{1}\partial_{\theta}(v_{2}-v_{1})

\displaystyle+(v_{2}-v_{1})r\partial_{r}v_{2}+v_{1}r\partial_{r}(v_{2}-v_{1})+(u_{1}+u_{2})(u_{1}-u_{2}).

Thus, following the estimate (
4.28
) line by line, we obtain (
4.29
).
Hence, there exist
\varepsilon_{0}>0,\eta_{0}>0
such that for any
\varepsilon\in(0,\varepsilon_{0}),\eta\in(0,\eta_{0})
, the operator

\displaystyle(u,v)\mapsto(\bar{u},\bar{v})

maps the ball
\{(u,v):\|(u,v)\|^{2}_{Y}\leq 2C^{3}\varepsilon^{6}\}
in
Y
into itself and is a contraction mapping. This complete the proof of this proposition.

MM

\square

Now we can give the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Proof.

Combining Proposition
4.3
and the approximate solution (
2.181
)-(
2.183
) of the Navier-Stokes equations (
1
), we easily obtain Theorem
1.2
.

MM

\square

5.
Appendix

Appendix A: Constant coefficient periodic PDE

In this appendix we give a brief argument to solve the following problem which also appeared in Appendix A in
[
6
]
,

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}(Q_{0})_{\theta}=u_{e}(1)(Q_{0})_{\psi\psi},\\[5.0pt]
Q_{0}(\theta,\psi)=Q_{0}(\theta+2\pi,\psi),\\[5.0pt]
Q_{0}\big|_{\psi=0}=f_{\eta}(\theta),\ Q_{0}\big|_{\psi\rightarrow-\infty}=0,\end{array}\right.

where

\displaystyle f_{\eta}(\theta)=\alpha^{2}+2\alpha\eta f(\theta)+\eta^{2}f^{2}(\theta)-u_{e}^{2}(1)=2\alpha\eta f(\theta)+\eta^{2}f^{2}(\theta)-\frac{\eta^{2}}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}f^{2}(\theta)d\theta.

Let
Q_{0}(\theta,\psi)=\sum\limits_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{ik\theta}Q_{0k}(\psi)
and substitute it into (
5
), we obtain

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}ikQ_{0k}=u_{e}(1)Q_{0k}^{\prime\prime},\\[5.0pt]
Q_{0k}\big|_{\psi=0}=\widehat{f_{\eta}}(k)=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}e^{-ik\theta}f_{\eta}(\theta)d\theta,\\[5.0pt]
Q_{0k}\big|_{\psi\rightarrow-\infty}=0.\end{array}\right.

It is easy to get

\displaystyle Q_{0k}(\psi)=\widehat{f_{\eta}}(k)e^{\alpha_{k}\psi}

with

\alpha_{k}=\sqrt{\frac{|k|}{2u_{e}(1)}}(1+\text{sgn}k\cdot i)
. Then

\displaystyle Q_{0}(\theta,\psi)=\sum\limits_{k\in\mathbb{Z}}e^{ik\theta}\widehat{f_{\eta}}(k)e^{\alpha_{k}\psi}\in X

and

\displaystyle\|Q_{0}\|_{X}\leq C\eta.

(5.6)

Appendix B: Construction of corrector
h(\theta,r)

In this section, we give a construction of corrector
h(\theta,r)
defined in subsection
2.5
. Firstly, we give a simple lemma which is similar to Lemma 6.1 in Appendix B in
[
6
]
.

Lemma 5.1
.

Assume that
K(\theta,r)
is a
2\pi
-periodic smooth function which satisfies

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}K(\theta,r)d\theta=0,\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1];\quad K(\theta,r_{0})=K(\theta,1)=0,

then there exists a
2\pi
-periodic function
h(\theta,r)
such that

\displaystyle\partial_{\theta}h(\theta,r)=K(\theta,r);\ h(\theta,r_{0})=h(\theta,1)=0;

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}h(\theta,r)d\theta=0,\ \|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k}h\|_{2}\leq C\|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k}K\|_{2}.

(5.7)

Proof.

Let

\displaystyle K(\theta,r)=\sum_{n\neq 0}K_{n}(r)e^{in\theta},\quad K_{n}(r_{0})=K_{n}(1)=0.

Set

\displaystyle h(\theta,r)=\sum_{n\neq 0}\frac{K_{n}(r)}{in}e^{in\theta}.

It’s easy to justify that
h(\theta,r)
satisfies (
5.1
) which complete the proof.

MM

\square

Next, we construct the corrector
h(\theta,r)
by the above lemma.
Direct computation gives

\displaystyle u^{a}_{\theta}+(rv^{a})_{r}=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{\theta}h(\theta,r)+K(\theta,r),

where

\displaystyle K(\theta,r)=

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4}\chi(r)[Y\partial_{Y}v_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Y)+v_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Y)]

\displaystyle+\varepsilon^{4}(1-\chi(r))[Z\partial_{Z}\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Z)+\hat{v}_{p}^{(4)}(\theta,Z)]

\displaystyle+r\chi^{\prime}(r)\Big(\sum_{i=1}^{4}\varepsilon^{i}v_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Y)-\sum_{i=1}^{4}\varepsilon^{i}\hat{v}_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Z)\Big).

Notice that
\chi^{\prime}(r)=0,\ r\in[r_{0},r_{1}]\cup[r_{2},1]
and the property of
(v_{p}^{(i)},\hat{v}_{p}^{(i)})
, we know that
K(\theta,r)=O(\varepsilon^{4})
and

\displaystyle K(\theta,1)=0,\ K(\theta,r_{0})=0.

Moreover, notice that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}v_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Y)d\theta=0,\ \forall\ Y\leq 0;\quad\int_{0}^{2\pi}\hat{v}_{p}^{(i)}(\theta,Z)d\theta=0,\ \forall\ Z\geq 0,\quad i=1,\cdot\cdot\cdot,4,

we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{2\pi}K(\theta,r)d\theta=0,\ \forall r\in[r_{0},1].

Thus, we can choose
h(\theta,r)
by Lemma
5.1
such that

\displaystyle\varepsilon^{4}\partial_{\theta}h(\theta,r)+K(\theta,r)=0,\ h(\theta,1)=0,\ h(\theta,r_{0})=0,\ \|\partial_{\theta}^{j}\partial_{r}^{k}h\|_{2}\leq C\varepsilon^{-k}.

Appendix C: Generalized Prandtl-Batchelor theory in an annulus.

In this appendix, we derive a generalized Prandtl-Batchelor theory for the forced steady Navier-Stokes equations on a two-dimensional
annulus. For Prandtl-Batchelor theory on a simply connected
domain, we refer to
[
1
,
22
,
23
]
or Appendix C in
[
6
]
.

Consider the two-dimensional forced Navier-Stokes equations (
1.1
).
Let
w^{\varepsilon}=u^{\varepsilon}_{y}-v^{\varepsilon}_{x}
be the vorticity, then it’s easy to obtain that

\displaystyle(v^{\varepsilon},-u^{\varepsilon})^{T}w^{\varepsilon}+\nabla\Big(p^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\Big)=\varepsilon^{2}(\triangle\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}+\mathbf{F}).

Let
\Phi^{\varepsilon}(x,y)
be the stream function which satisfies
\partial_{y}\Phi^{\varepsilon}=u^{\varepsilon},-\partial_{x}\Phi^{\varepsilon}=v^{\varepsilon}
. Assuming that

\displaystyle\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{c}:=\{(x,y)|\Phi^{\varepsilon}(x,y)=c\}

is a closed curve for any
c\in\Phi^{\varepsilon}(\Omega)
.
By integrating the Navier-Stokes equations over the closed curve
\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{c}
we deduce that

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{c}}\Big((v^{\varepsilon},-u^{\varepsilon})^{T}w^{\varepsilon}+\nabla\Big(p^{\varepsilon}+\frac{1}{2}|\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}|^{2}\Big)\Big)d\vec{\tau}=0,

thus we obtain that

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma^{\varepsilon}_{c}}(\triangle\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}+\mathbf{F})d\vec{\tau}=0.

Assume that
\mathbf{u}^{\varepsilon}\rightarrow\mathbf{u}_{e}
in some strong sense and
\mathbf{u}_{e}
is the solution of forced steady Euler equations, then there holds

\displaystyle\int_{\Gamma_{c}^{e}}(\triangle\mathbf{u}_{e}+\mathbf{F})d\vec{\tau}=0,

(5.8)

where
\Gamma_{c}^{e}
is the stream line of the Euler flow.

If the Euler flow
\mathbf{u}_{e}
has no stagnation point in the annulus, then it’s a rotating shear flow
\mathbf{u}_{e}=u_{e}(r)\vec{e}_{\theta}
and the stream line is circle, see
[
15
]
. From this and (
5.8
), we deduce that

\displaystyle u^{\prime\prime}_{e}(r)+\frac{1}{r}u^{\prime}_{e}(r)-\frac{1}{r^{2}}u_{e}(r)=-\bar{F}_{u}(r),

(5.9)

where
F_{u}(\theta,r)=\mathbf{F}\cdot\vec{e}_{\theta}
and
\bar{F}_{u}(r):=\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}F_{u}(\theta,r)d\theta.

Similarly, for a finite channel
\{(x,y):x\in[0,2\pi],\ y_{0}\leq y\leq y_{1}\}
,
if the inviscid limit of Navier-Stokes flow is a shear flow
(u_{e}(y),0)^{T}
,
then there also holds

u^{\prime\prime}_{e}(y)+\frac{1}{2\pi}\int_{0}^{2\pi}F_{1}(x,y)dx=0,

where
F_{1}
is the first component of
\mathbf{F}
.
